Some vanishing results for the rational completed cohomology of Shimura varieties
Kai-Wen Lan and Lue Pan

Abstract
. Based on an almost Kodaira-type vanishing result in mixed characteristics of Bhatt, we show that, in the locally analytic completed cohomology of a general Shimura variety, sufficiently regular infinitesimal weights can only show up in the middle degree.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11F77, 11G18; Secondary 11F33, 14F17, 14F30

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Main result

Let
p
be a prime number. Emerton introduced the (
p
-adically) completed cohomology of locally symmetric spaces, and described it as “a suitable surrogate for a space of
p
-adic automorphic forms”
[
Eme14
]
. This paper studies the completed cohomology of Shimura varieties below the middle degrees. Let us briefly recall Emerton’s construction. Let
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})
be a Shimura datum. For a neat open compact subgroup
K\subset\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
with
{\mathbb{A}^{\infty}}
denoting the ring of finite adeles, let

\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q})\big\backslash\bigl(\mathsf{X}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})\bigr)\big/K

be the (complex analytic) Shimura variety of level
K
. Fix an open compact subgroup
K^{p}
of
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}})
, where
{\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}}
is the ring of finite adeles away from
p
. For each
i\geq 0
, the
i
-th completed cohomology is defined as

\tilde{H}^{i}:=\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{K_{p}}H^{i}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p},\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm an}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}).

It is
p
-adically complete and carries a natural continuous action of
\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
d
be the dimension of
\mathsf{X}
as a complex manifold. Similar to the cohomology of usual automorphic local systems, it is believed (see
[
CE12
]
) that
i=d
is the most interesting degree. Our main result supports this belief in terms of the infinitesimal character of the
\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action. To state it, we need to introduce some notation. Let
C
be the
p
-adic completion of an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}(C)
, and let
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
denote the center of the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. Fix a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
of
\mathfrak{g}
, denote by
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
the Weyl group of
\mathfrak{g}
with respect to
\mathfrak{h}
, and identify each character of
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
with a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit in the weight space
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
via the Harish-Chandra isomorphism. (Our convention is that the
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-action is centered at zero.) We say that a character
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
or its kernel (which defines a
C
-point of
\operatorname{Spec}Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
) is
sufficiently regular
if, for every weight
\lambda^{\prime}
in the corresponding
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit
[\lambda]
and every root
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{g}
with respect to
\mathfrak{h}
, we have

(1.1.1)

(\lambda^{\prime},{\alpha}^{\vee})\not\in\begin{cases}\{1,2\},&\text{if $\alpha$ is a shorter root, as in Definition \ref{def-shorter-rt}};\\
\{1\},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that the shorter root can only appear in
C
-simple factors of
\mathfrak{g}
of types
\mathrm{B}
and
\mathrm{C}
. When all
C
-simple factors of
\mathfrak{g}
are of types
\mathrm{A}
,
\mathrm{D}
, and
\mathrm{E}
, an irreducible representation of
\mathfrak{g}
of highest weight
\lambda
has sufficiently regular infinitesimal character if and only if
\lambda
is
regular
in the usual sense; i.e., not fixed by any nontrivial element of
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
. The set of non-sufficiently regular characters underlies a (reduced) closed subvariety of
\operatorname{Spec}Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
and defines an ideal
\mathcal{I}\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
.

Let
\tilde{H}_{C}^{i}:=\tilde{H}^{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
, and denote by
\tilde{H}_{C}^{i,\text{\rm la}}
its subspace of
\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-locally analytic vectors. There is a natural Lie algebra action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
\tilde{H}_{C}^{i,\text{\rm la}}
via derivation.

Theorem 1.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be as above. Then
\mathcal{I}^{n}
annihilates
(
i.e., acts by zero on
)

\tilde{H}_{C}^{<d,\text{\rm la}}
, for some
n>0
. In particular, if
[\lambda]:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
is sufficiently regular, then the
[\lambda]
-isotypic part
\tilde{H}_{C,[\lambda]}^{<d,\text{\rm la}}
is zero.

Remark 1.1.3
.

In fact, Theorem
1.1.2
will follow from a similar vanishing result in Corollary
6.1.4
under a slightly weaker regularity condition on the infinitesimal characters of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}
(rather than
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}(C)
), called “
\mu_{h}
-sufficient regularity”—See Definition
5.1.5
(
3
) for this notion, and see Definition
5.1.8
for the corresponding improvement of
\mathcal{I}\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
. As the name suggests, this regularity condition will depend on the choice of a Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{h}
. As we shall see in Example
5.1.7
, the
\mu_{h}
-sufficient regularity condition for a Shimura curve will be weaker than the one for the Hilbert modular variety associated with the same totally real field.

This result allows us to reprove the vanishing result for cohomology of automorphic local systems obtained by the first-named author in
[
Lan16
]
. See Remarks
5.1.9
and
6.1.6
below. As noted in
[
Lan16
]
, by using automorphic methods, Li and Schwermer
[
LS04
]
were able to prove the vanishing result only assuming that the highest weight of the local system is regular. It is not clear to us whether our method can reprove the same result when
\mathfrak{g}
has
C
-simple factors of types
\mathrm{B}
and
\mathrm{C}
. On the other hand, our method can also handle “big” local systems which are not necessarily finite-dimensional. See Corollary
6.1.7
.

We shall explain in Section
4.4
that the action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
on
\tilde{H}_{C}^{i,\text{\rm la}}
can be extended to an action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
, where
\mathfrak{m}
denotes a Levi subalgebra determined by the Hodge cocharacter, after fixing an isomorphism
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
of fields. The extra symmetry comes from the (partial) Sen operators on
\tilde{H}_{C}^{i,\text{\rm la}}
. Our actual main result, Theorem
6.1.3
, has a more refined version regarding this enlarged symmetry. Very roughly speaking, if we fix an infinitesimal character
[\lambda]
, it determines a list of possible Hodge–Tate–Sen weights corresponding to
\iota
, and the “sufficiently regular ones” among them will not show up in
\tilde{H}_{C,[\lambda]}^{<d,\text{\rm la}}
.

In a follow-up paper
[
Pan25a
]
, the second-named author shows that an extension of our method in the case of Hilbert modular varieties allows one to produce ideals of
U(\mathfrak{g})
(not just ideals of the center) annihilating
\tilde{H}_{C}^{<d,\text{\rm la}}
. We believe similar results should hold for more general Shimura varieties.

Remark 1.1.4
.

For each
i\leq d
, there should be a notion of
i
-sufficiently regularity for infinitesimal characters such that
\tilde{H}_{C,[\lambda]}^{<i,\text{\rm la}}=0
when
[\lambda]
is
i
-sufficiently regular. Our method can give results in this direction, but we shall not purse them in this paper. Similar to how we work out the sufficiently regular condition in Proposition
5.1.12
below, they should be reduced to combinatorial problems in Lie theory.

Remark 1.1.5
.

Since the pioneering work of Caraiani–Scholze
[
CS17
,
CS24
]
, there has been a lot of powerful vanishing results
[
Kos21
,
HL23
,
YZ25
]
concerning the cohomology of Shimura varieties below degree. There are two major differences between our work and these works. Firstly, these works allow torsion and hence integral coefficients, while our work is completely rational. Secondly, the genericity conditions in these works arise from the study of
p
-adic (or
\bmod~p
) representations of
\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
at a place
l\neq p
, while our work studies
p
-adic representations of
\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Remark 1.1.6
.

\tilde{H}^{>d}=0
. This is part of the Calegari–Emerton conjecture in
[
CE12
]
, and the first major breakthrough is made by Scholze in
[
Sch13b
,
Sch15
]
. Recently, the rational analogue
\tilde{H}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{>d}=0
for general Shimura varieties is proved in
[
RC22
]
, and the original
\tilde{H}^{>d}=0
for general Shimura varieties is proved in
[
He25
]
. See the references in these paper for important partial results in special cases.

1.2.
Overview of the proof

For simplicity of exposition, let us assume that the Shimura variety is compact. (Otherwise, we need to introduce toroidal compactifications.) Let us also assume that the derived group
\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm der}}
is simply-connected, and that the center
Z(\mathrm{G})
has the same rank over
\mathbb{Q}
and over
\mathbb{R}
. Fix a level
K_{p}
at
p
, and let
K=K^{p}K_{p}
. After choosing any isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
, consider the associated adic space
\mathcal{S}_{K}
of the Shimura variety over
C
. The tower of Shimura varieties
\{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}
forms a pro-étale
K_{p}
-torsor over
\mathcal{S}_{K}
. Denote by
\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
the space of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-valued locally analytic functions on
K_{p}
, viewed as a
K_{p}
-representation via left translation. It gives rise to an (infinite-dimensional) pro-étale local system
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
. By using the primitive comparison theorem, we obtain a natural isomorphism

\tilde{H}_{C}^{i,\text{\rm la}}\cong H_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{i}(\mathcal{S}_{K},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}).

Here comes the first main ingredient of the proof. Let
\mathcal{L}
be an ample line bundle on
\mathcal{S}_{K}
. We apply a Kodaira-type vanishing result in mixed characteristics of Bhatt’s
[
Bha
, Sec. 11.2]
and deduce that

H_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{<d}(\mathcal{S}_{K},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}}}\mathcal{L}^{-1})=0.

Now the question is how to untwist this
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
. For simplicity, let us temporarily write
\mathcal{F}={}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}
. We use an argument essentially due to Beilinson–Bernstein in their work on localization
[
BB81
]
. Choose a finite-dimensional representation
V
of
\mathrm{G}
, and consider

H_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{<d}(\mathcal{S}_{K},\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\otimes V)\cong H_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{<d}(\mathcal{S}_{K},\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}}}\mathcal{L}^{-1})\otimes V=0,

where the last isomorphism uses
\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes V\cong\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(K_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes V
, with
K_{p}
acting on every term except for the last
V
. By making suitable choices of
\mathcal{L}
and
V
under our simplifying assumptions, we can arrange that
\mathcal{F}
appears as a
direct summand
of
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\otimes V
on the sufficiently regular locus of
\operatorname{Spec}Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
. A more careful study of the
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
-action gives our main theorem.

Such an argument is probably unsurprising for people who are familiar with the Beilinson–Bernstein localization: there is a close relationship between twisting of a line bundle and the translation functor (defined as a direct summand of the functor of tensoring with a finite dimensional representation
[
BG80
]
) in the localization
[
BG99
]
. We remark that it was first observed in
[
Pan22
]
and further generalized in
[
RC22
]
that the sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is annihilated by certain first-order differential operators coming from geometric Sen theory, and hence can be regarded after pushing forward along the Hodge–Tate period map as some sort of localization of
\tilde{H}_{C}^{i,\text{\rm la}}
on the partial flag variety defined by the Hodge cocharacter.

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we discuss Bhatt’s Kodaira-type vanishing theorem, and make a slight generalization using some standard cyclic cover technique. In Section
3
, we recollect various standard facts about locally symmetric varieties and their compactifications, and about automorphic vector bundles and their canonical extensions. In Section
4
, we summarize some results of
[
RC22
]
on locally analytic functions over Shimura varieties at infinite levels. In Section
5
, under the simplifying assumptions that the derived group
\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm der}}
is simply-connected, and that the center
Z(\mathrm{G})
has the same rank over
\mathbb{Q}
and over
\mathbb{R}
, we carry out the translation functor argument sketched above, and explain where the sufficiently regular condition comes from. Finally, in Section
6
, we explain how to remove the simplifying assumptions by working with towers of closely related locally symmetric varieties.

Notation and conventions

We shall fix a prime number
p
. Let
C
be
p
-adic completion of an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{C}
. We shall denote by
D(\mathcal{O}_{C})^{a}
the almost derived category of quasi-coherent sheaves on
\mathcal{O}_{C}
with almost structure with respect to the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, as in
[
Bha
, Sec. 3.3]
.

For any algebraic group
\mathrm{H}
over a base field
k
and any
k
-algebra
l
, we shall denote by
\operatorname{Rep}_{l}(\mathrm{H})
the category of algebraic representations of
\mathrm{H}
(or rather its base extension
\mathrm{H}\otimes_{k}l
) over
l
.

For simplicity, we will often omit “
\otimes
” when denoting (possibly negative) tensor powers of line bundles.

2.
An almost Kodaira vanishing theorem of Bhatt

2.1.
Bhatt’s mixed characteristic Kodaira vanishing result

Recently, Bhargav Bhatt proved an almost Kodaira vanishing result over a mixed characteristic base. See
[
Bha
, Sec. 11.2]
. We will state (a special case of) his result below. Let us begin by introducing some notation.

Let
X
be a finitely presented flat
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-scheme. We shall denote by
X_{C}
its generic fiber; by
D_{\text{\rm cons}}^{b}(X_{C},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
the usual constructible derived category of étale
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-sheaves on
X_{C}
; by
\widehat{X}
the
p
-adic completion of
X
, viewed as a
p
-adic formal scheme; and by
\mathcal{X}
its adic generic fiber. We have a natural “support” map
\eta^{\prime}:\mathcal{X}\to X_{C}
induced by taking the supports of valuations, and a nearby cycles map
\nu^{\prime}:\mathcal{X}\to\widehat{X}
defined by taking the centers of valuations. We shall denote by
\eta:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to X_{C,\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
the morphism of étale sites induced by
\eta^{\prime}
, and by
\nu:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to\widehat{X}
the composition of
\nu^{\prime}
with the projection
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}
.

Theorem 2.1.1

(Bhatt)
.

Let
X
be a flat projective
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-scheme of relative dimension
d
, and
L
an ample line bundle on
X
. For any
F\in{{}^{p}}D_{\text{\rm cons}}^{\geq 0}(X_{C},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})

(
i.e., coconnective with respect to the perverse
t
-structure for middle perversity, as in
[
BBDG18
, §4]
)
, we have

R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}},(\eta^{*}F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1}\bigr)\in D^{\geq d}(\mathcal{O}_{C})^{a},

where the pullback
\nu^{*}
is understood as the pullback of a line bundle with respect to the integral structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}
on
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
; and where
\widehat{L}
denotes the
p
-adic formal completion of
L
, which is a line bundle on
\widehat{X}
.

Proof.

When
n=1
, this is Bhatt’s “reformulation of almost Kodaira vanishing via
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}
” in
[
Bha
, Sec. 11.2]
. The general case then follows by induction on
n\geq 1
.
∎

Corollary 2.1.2
.

Let
X
and
L
be as in Theorem
2.1.1
. Let
j:U\hookrightarrow X_{C}
be an open immersion from a smooth subvariety. For every étale finite
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-locally constant sheaf
F
on
U
, we have

R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}},(\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1}\bigr)\in D^{\geq d}(\mathcal{O}_{C})^{a}.

Proof.

Since
j:U\to X_{C}
is an open immersion, by
[
BBDG18
, Prop. 2.1.6]
,
Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}
is perverse
t
-left exact. Therefore,
Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}F[d]\in{{}^{p}}D_{\text{\rm cons}}^{\geq 0}(X_{C},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
, and we can apply Bhatt’s theorem.
∎

We will need a version of this corollary “completely on the generic fiber”. Let us explain the arguments as follows.

Construction 2.1.3
.

Let
X_{C}
be an algebraic variety over
C
, and
j:U\hookrightarrow X_{C}
an open immersion. Suppose
K
is a profinite group, and
\tilde{U}
is a pro-étale
K
-torsor over
U
. Concretely, this means that, for every open normal subgroup
H
of
K
, we have an étale
K/H
-torsor
U_{H}
over
U
; and for open normal subgroups
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
of
K
such that
H_{1}\subset H_{2}
, we have a
K
-equivariant map
U_{H_{1}}\to U_{H_{2}}
which satisfies usual compatibilities when having a tower of open normal subgroups.

Let
V
be a unitary
p
-adic Banach space representation of
K
. Then the unit ball
V^{\circ}
is
p
-adically complete and
K
-stable. For
n\geq 1
, every vector of
V_{n}:=V^{\circ}/p^{n}V^{\circ}
is fixed by an open subgroup of
K
. Hence, we can write

V_{n}=\cup_{M\subset V_{n}}\,M,

where
M
runs over all finite
K
-stable subgroups of
V_{n}
, and such
M
’s form a direct system. For each
M
, the action of
K
on it factors through a finite quotient
K/H
. By descending the constant étale sheaf associated with
M
along the
K/H
-torsor
U_{H}\to U
, we obtain a locally constant étale sheaf
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}
on
U
, which is independent of the choice of
H
. We define an étale sheaf

{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}:=\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M},

which we view as the étale local system associated with
V_{n}
. Note that the open immersion
j:U\to X_{C}
is quasi-compact. By abuse of notation, let us define
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}_{X_{C}}:=Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}

(
even though this is not a sheaf in general
)
, and define

{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{X_{C}}:=Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}\cong\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}.

Then
\{{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{X_{C}}\}_{n}
form a projective system as
\{{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}\}_{n}
does.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
denote its pro-étale site, introduced in
[
Sch13a
]
. Let

\eta_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to X_{C,\text{\rm\'{e}t}}

denote the composition of natural projections of sites. Still by abuse of notation, consider

(2.1.4)

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\eta_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{X_{C}},

and consider the following derived limit

(2.1.5)

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}:=R\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}=R\lim_{n}\eta_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}.

Since
V^{\circ}
is
p
-adically complete and torsionfree, it follows from the construction that there are exact sequences

(2.1.6)

0\to{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{m}}\xrightarrow{\cdot p^{n}}{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n+m}}\to{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}\to 0.

By applying
\eta_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}
to this, and by taking derived limit over
m
, we obtain

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}/p^{n}\cong{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}},

for each
n\geq 1
, where
/p^{n}
is understood in the derived sense; i.e.,
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathbb{L}}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
.

Remark 2.1.7
.

We emphasize that we are pushing forward along the algebraic open immersion
j
, which is quasi-compact, instead of the induced open immersion
\breve{j}
between adic spaces, which is not quasi-compact in general. Let
\eta:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to X_{C,\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
and
\eta_{U}:\mathcal{U}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to U_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
denote the canonical morphisms. Since
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}
is constructible for each finite
M
, by Huber’s comparison theorem
[
Hub96
, Thm. 3.8.1]
, we have

{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\eta^{*}{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{X_{C}}=\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}\cong\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}=\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}R\breve{j}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\eta_{U}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}.

Since
\breve{j}
might not be quasi-compact and
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}
might not be constructible either, we do not know whether
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is equal to
R\breve{j}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\eta_{U}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}\cong R\breve{j}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}\eta_{U}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}
.

Theorem 2.1.8
.

Let
X_{C}
be a normal projective variety over
C
of dimension
d
, and
L_{C}
a line bundle on
X_{C}
. Suppose that
X_{C}
and
L_{C}
are defined over a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
U\subset X_{C}
be a smooth open subvariety,
K
a profinite group, and
\tilde{U}
a pro-étale
K
-torsor of
U

(
as in Construction
2.1.3
)
. Assume that:

(2.1.9)

L_{C}^{m}
is globally generated, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}

(
i.e.,
L_{C}
is semiample
)
, whose global sections define a morphism
\phi_{m}:X_{C}\to\mathbb{P}H^{0}(X_{C},L_{C}^{m})
; and the restriction
\phi_{m}|_{U}:U\to\mathbb{P}H^{0}(X_{C},L_{C}^{m})
is an immersion.

Then, for every unitary
p
-adic Banach space representation
V
of
K
, we have

R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(C),

where
\eta_{\mathcal{X}}:(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}})\to(X_{C},\mathcal{O}_{X_{C}})
is the composition of the natural projection of ringed sites
(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}})\to(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}})
and
\eta
, and where
\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
denotes the
p
-adically completed tensor product. Then we can write:

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1}\cong\bigl(R\lim_{n}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p^{n}))\bigr)\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1}.

The assumption (
2.1.9
) in Theorem
2.1.8
holds if
L_{C}
is ample. The essential improvement here is that
L_{C}
is no longer assumed to be extendable to a (semiample) line bundle on some integral model of
X_{C}
(over
\mathcal{O}_{C}
). The rest of this subsection will be devoted to the proof of this theorem. Unsurprisingly, we will use some technique of cyclic coverings to reduce it to Bhatt’s result.

Construction 2.1.10

(cf.
[
Laz04
, Prop. 4.1.6]
)
.

Let
X
be a variety over a field of characteristic zero, and
L
a line bundle on
X
. Suppose
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and
s\in\Gamma(X,L^{m})
is a nonzero section, which defines a divisor
D\subset X
. Then there exists a finite flat covering
\pi:Y\to X
of degree
m
, where
Y
is a scheme such that
L^{\prime}:=\pi^{*}L
admits a section
s^{\prime}\in\Gamma(Y,L^{\prime})
satisfying
(s^{\prime})^{m}=\pi^{*}(s)
. Explicitly,
Y
is the relative spectrum of the quotient of the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra

\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(L^{-1}):=\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}L^{-i}=\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L^{-1}\oplus L^{-2}\oplus\cdots

by the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-ideal generated by
s(x)-x
, for all
x\in L^{-m}
, where
s
is viewed as a morphism
L^{-m}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
. By construction, there is a natural isomorphism

\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L^{-1}\oplus L^{-2}\oplus\cdots\oplus L^{-m+1}

of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. Moreover, there is a natural action of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
on
Y
, defined by

u\cdot a=u^{i}a,

for all
u\in\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
and
a\in L^{i}
, for all
i=0,-1,\ldots,-m+1
. Clearly,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
acts transitively on each fiber of
\pi
. If
X
and
D
are nonsingular, then so is
Y
.

The following proposition roughly says that it suffices to prove Theorem
2.1.8
after passing to a cyclic cover.

Proposition 2.1.11
.

Same setup as in Theorem
2.1.8
. Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
0\neq s\in\Gamma(X_{C},L_{C}^{m})
, so that we have a cyclic covering
\pi:Y\to X_{C}
as in Construction
2.1.10
. Let
\mathcal{Y}
denote the adic space associate with
Y
, and let
\breve{\pi}:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
denote the induced morphism. Let
U^{\prime}:=\pi^{-1}(U)
, and let
j^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to Y
denote the canonical open immersion.
(
We shall use superscripts
′
to denote objects on
Y
.
)
By forming fiber products, the pro-étale
K
-torsor
\tilde{U}
over
U
pulls back to a pro-étale
K
-torsor
\tilde{U}^{\prime}
over
U^{\prime}
. Let
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}
over
U_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
and
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\cong R\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
over
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
be as in Construction
2.1.3
. By applying Construction
2.1.3
to
j^{\prime}
,
\tilde{U}^{\prime}
, and
V^{\prime}=V
instead, we obtain
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}^{\prime}}
over
U_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}^{\prime}
and
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}=R\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
over
\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
. Then
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
naturally acts on
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\pi^{*}L_{C}^{-1}
; and there is a natural isomorphism

H^{i}(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\cong H^{i}(\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}},

for each
i\geq 0
. In particular, if
H^{i}(\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})=0
, then
H^{i}(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})=0
.

Proof.

By the projection formula, we have

\begin{split}&H^{i}(\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\\
&\cong H^{i}(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1}))\\
&\cong H^{i}(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}})\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1}).\end{split}

Since taking
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
-invariants is exact over characteristic zero base fields, and it suffices to show that the canonical morphism

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}\to\bigl(R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}})\bigr)^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}

is an isomorphism. Let us also abusively denote by
(\,\cdot\,)^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}
the operation of taking derived invariants, even when the coefficients are not over characteristic zero fields. Since
\pi
is proper, so is the associated morphism
\breve{\pi}
of adic spaces, and
R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}
commutes with inverting
p
. Therefore, it suffices to prove that the integral version

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}\to\bigl(R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+})\bigr)^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}

is an
almost isomorphism
, which then implies the above assertion by inverting
p
.

Since
R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}
commutes with derived limits by
[
Sta
,
Tag 0A07
]
, since
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}=R\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}=R\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
by construction, and since
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
are filtered by copies of
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, respectively, it suffices to show that the canonical morphism

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p)\to\bigl(R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p))\bigr)^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}

is an almost isomorphism. Since both
R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}
and tensor products commute with filtered colimits because
\pi
is proper, by writing
V_{1}^{\prime}=V_{1}
as a union of
K
-stable finite-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-subspaces, we may assume from now on that
V_{1}^{\prime}=V_{1}
is finite.

Let
\lambda:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
denote the canonical projection of sites, and let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p
denote the mod
p
structure sheaf on
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
. Then we have
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}\cong\lambda^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p\cong\lambda^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p)
, by
[
Sch13a
, Lem. 4.2(iii)]
. Similar statements hold over
\mathcal{Y}
. By
[
Sch13a
, Cor. 3.17]
, it suffices to show that the étale version

{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p)\to\bigl(R\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p))\bigr)^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}

is an almost isomorphism. Let
\eta^{\prime}:\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to Y_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
denote the canonical projection of sites. By construction,
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\cong\eta^{\prime*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{Y}
, where
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{Y}:=Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}
is a constructible
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-sheaf on
Y_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
by our assumption that
V_{1}^{\prime}=V_{1}
is finite. Since
\pi
is finite, by the primitive comparison theorem
[
Sch13b
, Thm. 3.13]
(although the almost version is sufficient for our purpose, see also
[
Zav25
, Cor. 7.2.9]
for a non-almost version in this case), the canonical morphism

(R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p)\to R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p))

is an almost isomorphism. Thus, it remains to show that the canonical morphism

{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}\to(R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}})^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}

is an isomorphism. Write
\pi^{\prime}=\pi|_{U^{\prime}}:U^{\prime}\to U
, so that
\pi\circ j^{\prime}=j\circ\pi^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to X_{C}
. By Huber’s comparison theorem (see
[
Hub96
, Prop. 2.1.4 and Thm. 3.8.1]
),

R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Y}}=R\breve{\pi}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\eta^{\prime*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}\cong\eta^{*}R\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}\cong\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}R\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}.

Since
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}_{\mathcal{X}}=\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}
, it suffices to show that the canonical morphism

{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}\to(R\pi_{*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}})^{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}}

over
U_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
is an isomorphism. By construction,
V^{\prime}=V
and
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}^{\prime}}\cong\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}^{\prime*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}
. Let
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{U}
(resp.
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{U^{\prime}}
) denote the constant sheaves associated with
\mathbb{F}_{p}
over
U
(resp.
U^{\prime}
). Since
\pi^{\prime}
is finite, by
[
Sta
,
Tag 0A4K
]
and the projection formula
[
Sta
,
Tag 0GL5
]
,
(\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{U^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}\cong\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}^{\prime*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}\cong R\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}^{\prime*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{1}}
. Thus, to complete the proof, it suffices to note that the canonical morphism
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{U}\to\pi_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}^{\prime}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{U^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism, because
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
acts transitively on each fiber of
\pi
, by construction.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1.8
.

First assume the following:

(2.1.12)

There is a finite morphism
\phi:X_{C}\to\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}
such that
\phi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}}(1)\cong L_{C}
.

We claim that there exists a finitely presented flat
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-integral model
X
of
X_{C}
, and
L_{C}
can be extended to an ample line bundle
L
on
X
. To see this, since
X_{C}
and
L_{C}
are defined over a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we may assume that
X_{C}\cong X_{E}\otimes_{E}C
for some variety
X_{E}
over
E
, and that
L_{C}
comes from an ample line bundle
L_{E}
over
X_{E}
. It suffices to produce integral models of
X_{E}
and
L_{E}
(over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
).

It follows from our assumption that there is finite morphism
\phi_{E}:X_{E}\to\mathbb{P}_{E}^{N}
. Consider the standard integral model of
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
of
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{E}
, and take its normalization
X_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
in
X_{E}
, as in
[
Sta
,
Tag 035H
]
. Since
X_{E}
is reduced and
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
is Nagata (by
[
Sta
,
Tag 0335
and
Tag 033S
]
), the natural morphism
\bar{\phi}:X_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\to\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{N}
is finite (by
[
Sta
,
Tag 03GH
]
). In particular, the pullback
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}:=\bar{\phi}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}(1)
is ample on
X_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
and extends
L_{E}
. Then we can take
X:=X_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}C
and
L:=L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}C
.

Recall that, in the beginning of the section, there are the morphism of ringed sites
\eta^{\prime}:(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\to(X_{C},\mathcal{O}_{X_{C}})
defined by taking the support of a valuation, and the nearby cycles map
\nu^{\prime}:(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{+})\to(\widehat{X},\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}})
defined by taking the center of a valuation. Then there is a natural isomorphism

(2.1.13)

\nu^{\prime*}\widehat{L}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{+}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\cong\eta^{\prime*}L_{C}

of invertible
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules on
\mathcal{X}
. Indeed, the case of
X=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}^{N}
and
L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
can be checked directly. The general case follows by pullback along
\bar{\phi}
.

As before, write
V_{n}=\cup_{M\subset V_{n}}\,M
as a union of finite
K
-stable subgroups. By Corollary
2.1.2
, for each such
M
, we have

R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}},(\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(\mathcal{O}_{C})^{a}.

By taking the directed limit over all such
M
and noting that both functors
\eta^{*}
and
Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}
commute with filtered colimits, we obtain

R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}},(\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(\mathcal{O}_{C})^{a}.

Recall that
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}=R\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, and each
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is the pullback of
\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}
via
\lambda:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
(see (
2.1.4
) and (
2.1.5
)). By
[
Sch13a
, Cor. 3.17]
and the above, and by taking derived limit over
n
, we obtain

R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(\mathcal{O}_{C})^{a},

where
\nu_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}:=\lambda\circ\nu:(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+})\to(\widehat{X},\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}})
. The pullback of the isomorphism (
2.1.13
) to the pro-étale site gives
\nu_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1}[\frac{1}{p}]\cong\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1}
. Since
\mathcal{X}
is quasi-compact,

R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\cong R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}^{*}\widehat{L}^{-1})[\tfrac{1}{p}]

belongs to
D^{\geq d}(C)
.

In general, we will use the following lemma to put ourselves in the situation where
\Gamma(X_{C},L_{C})
defines a finite morphism of
X_{C}
to
\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}
, for some
N\geq 0
.

Lemma 2.1.14
.

Let
X
be a projective variety over a field
E
of characteristic zero, and
U\subset X
a smooth open subvariety. Let
L
be a line bundle on
X
such that
L^{m}
is globally generated and
\phi_{m}|_{U}:U\to\mathbb{P}H^{0}(X,L^{m})
is an immersion, for some
m>0
. Then there exists a basis
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}
of
\Gamma(X,L^{m})
such that, if we denote by
\pi_{i}:Y_{i}\to X
the cyclic cover constructed from
s_{i}
, as in Construction
2.1.10
, for
i=0,\cdots,N
, and by
\pi:Y:=Y_{0}\times_{X}Y_{1}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y_{N}\to X
the fiber product of all
\pi_{i}
, then
\pi^{-1}(U)
is smooth and
Y
is reduced
(
i.e.,
Y
is a variety
)
.

Assuming this lemma, let us finish the proof of Theorem
2.1.8
. By assumption,
X_{C}
and
L_{C}
are defined over a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
m
be as in the theorem, so that we have global sections
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}
of
L_{C}^{m}
defined over
E
, and we can construct
Y_{0},\cdots,Y_{N}
and
\pi:Y\to X_{C}
as in the lemma. Let
L_{C}^{\prime}=\pi^{*}L_{C}
. By Construction
2.1.10
,
L_{C}^{\prime}
carries global sections
s_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,s_{N}^{\prime}
defined over
E
such that
(s_{i}^{\prime})^{m}=\pi^{*}s_{i}
, for
i=0,\ldots,N
. In particular,
s_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,s_{N}^{\prime}
induces a morphism
\phi^{\prime}:Y\to\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}
which fits into the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{m}}
\textstyle{{X_{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{m}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}}}

in which
\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}
is identified with
\mathbb{P}H^{0}(X_{C},L_{C}^{m})
using
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}
and
\nu_{m}
is defined by raising the homogeneous coordinates associated with
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}
to their
m
-th powers. Consider the open immersion
j^{\prime}:U^{\prime}:=\pi^{-1}(U)\to Y
. Since
\phi_{m}|_{U}
is an immersion and
\nu_{m}
is finite,
\phi^{\prime}|_{U^{\prime}}
is quasi-finite. Consider the Stein factorization of
\phi^{\prime}
given by
Y\xrightarrow{\phi^{\prime\prime}}Z\xrightarrow{g}\mathbb{P}^{N}
, where
g
is finite and
\phi^{\prime\prime}
has connected fibers. By
[
Sta
,
Tag 03H0
and
Tag 03GW
]
,
j^{\prime\prime}:=\phi^{\prime\prime}|_{U^{\prime}}:U^{\prime}\to Z
is an open immersion. Moreover, since
L_{C}^{\prime}\cong\phi^{\prime*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}}(1)
, it descends to the ample line bundle
L_{C}^{\prime\prime}:=g^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{C}^{N}}(1)
on
Z
. Note that
Y
and
\phi^{\prime}
are still defined over a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\mathcal{Z}
denote the adic space associated with
Z
; define
\eta_{\mathcal{Z}}:(\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}})\to(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
as in Theorem
2.1.8
; and define
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Z}}
by applying Construction
2.1.3
to
j^{\prime\prime}
, the pullback
\tilde{U}^{\prime\prime}
of
\tilde{U}
to
Z
, and
V^{\prime\prime}=V
. By what we have proved under the assumption (
2.1.12
),

(2.1.15)

R\Gamma(\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{Z}}^{*}(L_{C}^{\prime\prime})^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(C),

On the other hand, since
\phi^{\prime\prime}:Y\to Z
is proper (and
V^{\prime\prime}=V^{\prime}=V
), by the same arguments based on Scholze’s primitive comparison theorem
[
Sch13b
, Thm. 3.13]
in the proof of Proposition
2.1.11
, there is a natural isomorphism

{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{Z}}^{*}(L_{C}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\cong R\phi_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t},*}^{\prime\prime}({}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*}(L_{C}^{\prime})^{-1}).

over
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
. Thus, it follows from (
2.1.15
) that

R\Gamma(\mathcal{Y}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\prime\circ}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*}(L_{C}^{\prime})^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(C).

Since
\pi:Y\to X
is the composition
Y=Y_{0}\times_{X}Y_{1}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y_{N}\to Y_{0}\times_{X}Y_{1}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y_{N-1}\to\cdots\to Y_{0}\to X
of cyclic covering maps pulled back from the
\pi_{i}
’s, we can finish the proof of Theorem
2.1.8
by repeatedly applying Proposition
2.1.11
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.1.14
.

One can argue as in the proof of
[
Laz04
, Thm. 4.1.10]
(the so-called
Bloch–Gieseker coverings
). For the convenience of the reader, let us give a more direct proof in our case. For any nonzero
s\in\Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{E}^{N},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{E}^{N}}(1))
, we shall denote by
H_{s}
the hyperplane it defines in
\mathbb{P}_{E}^{N}
. By repeatedly applying Bertini’s theorem, there exist nonzero
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}\in\Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{E}^{N},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{E}^{N}}(1))
such that, for
i=0,\cdots,N
:

•

H_{s_{i}}
does not contain any irreducible component of
\phi_{m}(X)
; and

•

H_{s_{i}}
intersects
\phi_{m}(U)\cap\big(\cap_{j\in I}H_{s_{j}}\bigr)
transversally, for any
I\subset\{0,\cdots,i-1\}
.

Construct the finite cover
\pi:Y\to X
as in the lemma. We first prove that
Y
is reduced. Since this is a local property, we may assume that
X=\operatorname{Spec}(A)
is affine and that
L
is trivial, and identify
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}
with elements in
A
. Then
Y\cong\operatorname{Spec}(B)
, where
B=A[x_{0},\ldots,x_{N}]/(x_{0}^{m}-s_{0},\ldots,x_{N}^{m}-s_{N})
. We may enlarge
E
so that
x^{m}-1
splits completely in
E
. Note that
G=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}\times\cdots\times\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
(
(N+1)
-copies of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{m}
) acts on
B
via
(a_{0},\cdots,a_{N})\cdot x_{i}=a_{i}x_{i}
. Let
f\in B
be a nilpotent element which is also an eigenvector of
G
. Then
f=ax_{0}^{i_{0}}\cdots x_{N}^{i_{N}}
for some
a\in A
and
i_{0},\cdots,i_{N}\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}
. Since
f^{m}=a^{m}s_{0}^{i_{0}}\cdots s_{N}^{i_{N}}\in A
is nilpotent, but
A
is reduced and
s_{0},\cdots,s_{N}
are nonzero, we must have
a=0
and hence
f=0
.

To see the smoothness of
\pi^{-1}(U)
, we may similarly assume that
U=\operatorname{Spec}(A)
and
\pi^{-1}(U)\cong\operatorname{Spec}(B)
, where
B=A[x_{0},\cdots,x_{N}]/(x_{0}^{m}-s_{0},\ldots,x_{N}^{m}-s_{N})
. Let
\mathfrak{m}
be a maximal ideal of
B
, and
\mathfrak{m}_{A}:=\mathfrak{m}\cap A
. Let
I
be the set of
i
’s such that
s_{i}\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}
. By our transversality assumption, we can find elements
t_{1},\cdots,t_{l}\in\mathfrak{m}_{A}
, with
l=\dim(A)-|I|
, such that the images of
\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{l}\}
and
\{s_{i}\}_{i\in I}
form a basis of
\mathfrak{m}_{A}/\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{2}
. Since
x_{i}^{m}-s_{i}
in
B
, for each
i\in I
, and since
m\in E^{\times}
, the images of
\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{l}\}
and
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}
also form a basis of
\mathfrak{m}/\mathfrak{m}^{2}
. Thus,
B
is smooth at
\mathfrak{m}
.
∎

2.2.
Reformulation via Kummer étale site

Instead of working with the complexes
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{X_{C}}
and
{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}
introduced in Construction
2.1.3
, we shall make use of the Kummer étale and pro-Kummer étale sites introduced in
[
DLLZ23a
]
and work instead with sheaves (i.e., complexes concentrated in single degrees). Our setup is as follows. Let
X_{C}
denote a smooth variety over
C
, and
D\subset X_{C}
an effective divisor with
normal crossings
, with complementary open immersion
j:U\to X
. Let
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{D}
denote the rigid analytic varieties over
C
associated with
X_{C}
. Then
\mathcal{D}
is also a normal crossings divisor on
\mathcal{X}
, with complementary open immersion
\breve{j}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{X}
, which defines an fs log structure on
\mathcal{X}
whose restriction to
\mathcal{U}
is the trivial one, as explained in
[
DLLZ23a
, Ex. 2.3.16]
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}
denote its Kummer étale site with integral structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}}^{+}
, as in
[
DLLZ23a
, Def. 4.1.16 and 4.3.1]
, and let
g:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
the canonical morphism induced by forgetting the log structure. The restriction
g_{\mathcal{U}}:\mathcal{U}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{U}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
is trivially an isomorphism, and the canonical morphisms of sites
\breve{j}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}:\mathcal{U}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
and
\breve{j}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}:\mathcal{U}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}
satisfy
g\circ\breve{j}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}=\breve{j}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
.

Proposition 2.2.1
.

Let
F
be an étale
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-local system of finite rank over
U
.

(1)

Purity:
Rj_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}F=j_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}F
is a torsion Kummer étale local system.

(2)

There is a natural isomorphism

(Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}\cong(Rg_{*}j_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}\cong Rg_{*}(j_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}F\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}}^{+}).

Proof.

See
[
DLLZ23a
, Lem. 4.5.8 and Thm. 4.6.1]
. Note that
(Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}\cong(Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p^{n})
, because
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
∎

Construction 2.2.2
.

Suppose
K
is a profinite group, and
\tilde{U}
is a pro-étale
K
-torsor over
U
, as in Construction
2.1.3
. Let
V
be a unitary
p
-adic Banach space representation of
K
. As in Construction
2.1.3
,
V_{n}=V^{\circ}/p^{n}=\cup_{M\subset V_{n}}\,M
, where
M
runs over all finite
K
-stable subgroups of
V_{n}
; and for each
M
, we constructed an étale sheaf
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}
over
U
. Let
\eta_{U}:\mathcal{U}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\to U_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
denote the canonical morphism of sites. Over
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}
, we define
{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{M}_{\mathcal{X}}:=R\breve{j}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}\eta_{U}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}
, for each
M
as above; and define
{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{M}_{\mathcal{X}}
, which can be thought as the associated Kummer étale local systems.
(
These are sheaves, not just complexes.
)
Let
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}
denote the pro-Kummer-étale site introduced in
[
DLLZ23a
, Def. 5.1.2]
, and let
\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}
denote the projection of sites. We define pro-Kummer étale sheaves
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
,
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\varprojlim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, and
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}_{\mathcal{X}}:={}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, which are the pro-Kummer étale local systems associated with
V_{n}
,
V^{\circ}
, and
V
, respectively.

The projective limit in Construction
2.2.2
agrees with the derived limit, by the following lemma.

Lemma 2.2.3
.

In Construction
2.2.2
, assume moreover that everything is defined over a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

(
as in
[
DLLZ23a
, Sec. 5.3]
)
. Then:

(1)

R^{i}\lim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}=0
, for
i>0
.

(2)

{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}/p^{n}\cong{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}
, for each
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Starting with the short exact sequence (
2.1.6
), by Proposition
2.2.1
(
1
) and by expressing
V_{n+m}
as the direct limit of its finite stable
K
-subgroups, we obtain a short exact sequence

0\to{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{m}}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\cdot p^{n}}{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n+m}}_{\mathcal{X}}\to{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}\to 0.

The same holds for its pullback by
\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}
. Taking the inverse limit over
m
, we see that (
1
) implies (
2
). It remains to prove (
1
). Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
be a universal cover of a log affinoid perfectoid object of
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}
, as in the proof of
[
DLLZ23a
, Prop. 5.3.13]
. Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
is quasi-compact, for all
i\geq 0
, by using
[
Sta
,
Tag 0739
]
, we have

(2.2.4)

\begin{split}H^{i}({\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}_{/\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}})&\cong H^{i}({\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}_{/\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}},\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\breve{j}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}\eta_{U}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M})\\
&\cong\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}H^{i}({\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}_{/\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}},\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\breve{j}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t},*}\eta_{U}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}),\end{split}

where
M
runs through all finite
K
-stable subgroups of
V_{n}
. By
[
DLLZ23a
, Prop. 5.3.13]
, (
2.2.4
) is zero when
i>0
. Therefore, for all
n\geq 1
, the natural map

H^{0}({\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}_{/\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{X}})\to H^{0}({\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}_{/\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}})

is surjective; and
R^{1}\lim_{n}H^{0}({\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}_{/\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}})=0
. Since such
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
form a basis of
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}
, by
[
DLLZ23a
, Prop. 5.3.12]
, (
1
) follows, by
[
Sch13a
, Lem. 3.18]
.
∎

The following is the Kummer version of Theorem
2.1.8
.

Corollary 2.2.5
.

Let
X_{C}
be a smooth equidimensional projective variety over
C
of dimension
d
, and
L_{C}
a line bundle on
X_{C}
. Let
U\subset X_{C}
be a smooth open subvariety whose complement
D
is an effective divisor of normal crossings, so that we have the associated fs log adic spaces
\mathcal{X}
etc as in the beginning of this Section
2.2
. Let
K
be a profinite group, and
\tilde{U}
a pro-étale
K
-torsor over
U
. Assume that the same condition
(
2.1.9
)
as in Theorem
2.1.8
holds. Assume moreover that
X_{C}
,
U
, and
\tilde{U}
are defined over a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then, for every unitary
p
-adic Banach space representation
V
of
K
, to which Construction
2.2.2
applies, we have

\begin{split}&R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\nu_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\\
&\cong R\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\in D^{\geq d}(C),\end{split}

where
\nu_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}:(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}})\to(X_{C},\mathcal{O}_{X_{C}})
is the natural projection of ringed sites, where
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}
denotes the completed structure sheaf
(
as in
[
DLLZ23a
, Def. 5.4.1]
)
, and where
\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
denotes
p
-adically completed tensor product.

Remark 2.2.6
.

The condition (
2.1.9
) in Theorem
2.1.8
and Corollary
2.2.5
holds if there is a morphism
\pi:X_{C}\to Y_{C}
of projective varieties such that
\pi|_{U}:U\to Y_{C}
is an open immersion and such that
L_{C}^{m}
descends to an ample line bundle on
Y_{C}
. In applications,
X_{C}
will be some projective smooth toroidal compactification of a Shimura variety, and
Y_{C}
will be the associated minimal compactification.

Proof of Corollary
2.2.5
.

In view of Theorem
2.1.8
, it suffices to show that

Rg_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t},*}({}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\nu_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1})\cong{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V^{\circ}}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}L_{C}^{-1},

where
g_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}
denotes the canonical projection
\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
. Since
Rg_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t},*}
commutes with derived limit, by the projection formula, it suffices to show that

(2.2.7)

Rg_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t},*}\bigl((\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p^{n})\bigr)\cong{}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p^{n}).

By definition (see
[
DLLZ23a
, Def. 5.4.1]
), we have
(\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\,{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p^{n})\cong\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\bigl({}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}}^{+}/p^{n})\bigr)\cong\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}}^{+})
. By
[
DLLZ23a
, Prop. 5.2.1]
, the left-hand side of (
2.2.7
) is the pullback of
Rg_{*}({}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}}^{+})
via the natural projection
\lambda:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
. On the other hand, the right-hand side of (
2.2.7
) is the pullback of
(\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}
via
\lambda
. Hence, it suffices to prove that

Rg_{*}({}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}}^{+})\cong(\eta^{*}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}}^{+}.

When
V_{n}
is finite, this follows from Proposition
2.2.1
and Huber’s comparison theorem
[
Hub96
, Thm. 3.8.1]
. In general, we have
Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V_{n}}=\varinjlim_{M\subset V_{n}}Rj_{\text{\rm\'{e}t},*}\,{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{M}
, with
M
running through all finite
K
-stable subgroups of
V_{n}
, as usual; and it remains to observe that
Rg_{*}
commutes with filtered colimits, as
g:\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}
is qcqs, and that tensor product commutes with colimits
[
Sta
,
Tag 03EP
]
.
∎

3.
Locally symmetric varieties and automorphic vector bundles

In this section, we study (disjoint unions of) locally symmetric varieties and their minimal and toroidal compactifications, and explain materials that will be useful for our study of completed cohomology of Shimura varieties in later sections.

3.1.
General setup

We shall consider pairs
(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})
of the following type.

•

\mathrm{H}
is a connected reductive algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. We shall denote its center by
Z(\mathrm{H})
, and consider the associated adjoint group
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}:=\mathrm{H}/Z(\mathrm{H})
.

•

\mathsf{Y}
is a finite disjoint union of Hermitian symmetric domains such that
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})
acts transitively on
\mathsf{Y}
via its image under
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})
, and such that the
neutral component
(or
identity component
; i.e., the connected component containing the identity element)
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})
acts with maximal compact stabilizer subgroups. (Since
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
is a connected semisimple Lie group, this is consistent with the classification of their associated Hermitian symmetric domains, which we allow to be trivial when the Lie group is compact, as in
[
Hel01
]
. See also
[
Del79
, Sec. 1.2]
and
[
Mil05
, Sec. 1]
.)

Let
y_{0}\in\mathsf{Y}
be any fixed choice of a point of
\mathsf{Y}
, and let
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
be the connected component of
\mathsf{Y}
containing
y_{0}
. For any group
A
acting continuous on
\mathsf{Y}
, we shall denote by
A_{+}
the stabilizer
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
in
A
; i.e., the subgroup of
A
consisting of elements of
A
stabilizing
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
. In particular, we have
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
, the stabilizer of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
in
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})
, so that
\mathsf{Y}^{+}\cong\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}/K_{\infty}\cong\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}/K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}}
, where
K_{\infty}
(resp.
K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}}
, by abuse of notation) is the stabilizer of
y_{0}
in
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
(resp.
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
). By our assumption above,
K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. We shall extend this notation system by adding subscripts “
+
” to stabilizers of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
in other groups acting on
\mathsf{Y}
.

Since the underlying Riemannian symmetric space of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
parameterizes Cartan decompositions, the point
y_{0}\in\mathsf{Y}^{+}
induces a decomposition

(3.1.1)

\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{-}\oplus(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty})_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{+},

where
(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{+}
(resp.
(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{-}
) is the holomorphic (resp. anti-holomorphic) tangent space of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
at
y_{0}
; and the complex structure of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
at the point
y_{0}
fixed by
K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}}
defines a homomorphism
u:\mathrm{U}_{1}:=\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}:|z|=1\}\to K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
(as in
[
Mil05
, Thm. 1.21 and Cor. 1.22]
), whose complexification gives a homomorphism

(3.1.2)

\mu^{\text{\rm ad}}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}.

By construction, (
3.1.1
) is a decomposition into spaces of weights
-1
,
0
, and
1
for the representation of
\mathbb{C}
defined by the composition of
\operatorname{Lie}\mu^{\text{\rm ad}}:\mathbb{C}\to\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
with the canonical splitting
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\cong[\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}},\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}]\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}
; and there are no other weights. This implies, in particular, that both
(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{+}
and
(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{-}
are abelian unipotent Lie subalgebras of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}
normalized by the adjoint actions of
(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty})_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Let
\mathrm{M}
,
\mathrm{N}
, and
\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
be connected subgroups of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}
such that (
3.1.1
) induce
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty})_{\mathbb{C}}
,
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{+}
, and
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(T_{y_{0}}\mathsf{Y}^{+})_{\mathbb{C}}^{-}
. Then

(3.1.3)

\mathrm{P}:=\mathrm{N}\mathrm{M}\cong\mathrm{N}\rtimes\mathrm{M}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}:=\mathrm{M}\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}\cong\mathrm{M}\ltimes\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}

are two opposite parabolic subgroups with unipotent radicals
\mathrm{N}
and
\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
, respectively; and
\mathrm{M}
is a common Levi subgroup of
\mathrm{P}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}
. In
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{C})
, we have
K_{\infty}=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}\cap\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}(\mathbb{C})=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}\cap\mathrm{P}(\mathbb{C})
. Then we also have the following:

Remark 3.1.4
.

Given any
W
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P})
(resp.
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}})
), the action of the composition of
\operatorname{Lie}\mu^{\text{\rm ad}}
with
(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty})_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{M}
defines an increasing (resp. decreasing) filtration on
W
by subrepresentations of
\mathrm{P}
(resp.
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}
).

Over
\mathbb{C}
, we have two
partial flag varieties

(3.1.5)

\mathrm{Fl}:=\mathrm{P}\backslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}}:=\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}/\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}},

with natural transitive right and left actions of
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
, respectively. (It is a matter of conventions that we choose to have right and left actions as above. We can easily flip our choices by taking inverses.) By definition, the assignments of the decomposition (
3.1.1
) and of parabolic subgroups to
y_{0}\in\mathsf{Y}^{+}
are
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
-equivariant. Accordingly, we have the
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
-equivariant
Borel embedding

(3.1.6)

\mathsf{Y}^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{C})/\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}(\mathbb{C})\cong(\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}})^{\text{\rm an}}

from a Hermitian symmetric domain to its compact dual (cf.
[
BJ06
, Prop. I.5.24]
). Note that it is the complex analytic
(\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}})^{\text{\rm an}}
that serves as targets of Borel embeddings, while the
p
-adic analytification (i.e., associated adic space) of
\mathrm{Fl}_{C}
will serve as targets of Hodge–Tate period morphisms (to be explained in Section
4.2
).

Let
\mathrm{H}^{c}
be the quotient of
\mathrm{H}
by the minimal subtorus
Z_{s}(\mathrm{H})
of
Z(\mathrm{H})
such that the torus
Z(\mathrm{H})^{\circ}/Z_{s}(\mathrm{H})
has the same split ranks over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{R}
. For any closed algebraic subgroup
\mathrm{A}
of
\mathrm{H}
, we denote its closed (schematic) image in
\mathrm{H}^{c}
by
\mathrm{A}^{c}
. In particular, we have algebraic subgroups
\mathrm{M}^{c}
,
\mathrm{P}^{c}
, and
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c}
of
\mathrm{H}^{c}
. For
?=\emptyset
and
std
, since
\mathrm{N}^{?}
intersects
\ker(\mathrm{H}\to\mathrm{H}^{c})\subset Z(\mathrm{H})
trivially, the canonical short exact sequence
1\to\mathrm{N}^{?}\to\mathrm{P}^{?}\to\mathrm{M}\to 1
induces a canonical short exact sequence
1\to\mathrm{N}^{?}\to\mathrm{P}^{?,c}\to\mathrm{M}^{c}\to 1
. Similarly, for any subgroup
A
of
\mathrm{H}(R)
, where
R
is any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, we denote its image in
\mathrm{H}^{c}(R)
by
A^{c}
. In particular, the image of any open compact subgroup
K\subset\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
in
\mathrm{H}^{c}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
is denoted by
K^{c}
.

3.2.
Arithmetic quotients and double coset spaces

Let
(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})
be any pair as in Section
3.1
, together with
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}
,
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
, etc introduced there.

Let
\Gamma
be any
neat
arithmetic subgroup of
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
(see, e.g.,
[
Bor19
, 7.11, 17.1, and 17.3]
), whose action on
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
factors through its image
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
in
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
. By
[
Bor19
, Thm. 8.9, Rem. 8.11, and 17.3]
,
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
is again a neat arithmetic subgroup. Note that
\Gamma
acts on
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
via
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
, and the neatness of
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
implies that the finite discrete group
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}\cap K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is trivial, in which case
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
acts freely on
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
. Hence, the quotient

(3.2.1)

\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{\text{\rm an}}:=\Gamma\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}\cong\mathrm{S}^{\text{\rm an}}_{\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}}:=\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}

is a complex manifold (which we shall view as a smooth complex analytic space) as
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
is. Since
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})
acts on
\mathsf{Y}
via its image in
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})
, the whole center
Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{R})
acts trivially and hence lies in
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
. Therefore, we have
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}\cong\Gamma/\bigl(\Gamma\cap(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)
.

For any open compact subgroup
K\subset\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, consider the double coset space

(3.2.2)

\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}:=\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y}):=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})\big\backslash\bigl(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})\bigr)\big/K,

where
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
acts diagonally on
\mathsf{Y}\times\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
from the left-hand side, and where
K
acts only on
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
from the right-hand side. Since
\mathrm{H}
is connected as an algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
, by a special case of weak approximation (see
[
PR94
, Sec. 7.3, Thm. 7.7]
),
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
is dense in
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})
in the real topology. Consider
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}:=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
, which is the stabilizer of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
in
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
. Then we can rewrite (
3.2.2
) as

(3.2.3)

\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\big\backslash\bigl(\mathsf{Y}^{+}\times\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})\bigr)\big/K.

For each
h\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, let us introduce the subspace

(3.2.4)

\mathrm{S}_{K,h}^{\text{\rm an}}:=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\big\backslash\bigl(\mathsf{Y}^{+}\times(\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}hK)\bigr)\big/K

of
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}
. Since
\gamma hK=hK
for
\gamma\in\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
exactly when
\gamma\in hKh^{-1}
, we can write

(3.2.5)

\mathrm{S}_{K,h}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K,h}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\Gamma_{K,h}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}

instead of (
3.2.4
), which is a connected topological space as
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
is, where

(3.2.6)

\Gamma_{K,h}:=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap(hKh^{-1})

is an
arithmetic subgroup
of
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
, whose action on
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
factors through its image

(3.2.7)

\begin{split}\Gamma_{K,h}^{\text{\rm ad}}&\cong\Gamma_{K,h}\big/\bigl(\Gamma_{K,h}\cap(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)\\
&\cong\bigl(\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap(hKh^{-1})\bigr)\big/\bigl((Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\cap(hKh^{-1})\bigr)\end{split}

in
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
, which is an arithmetic subgroup of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
. For simplicity, when
h=1
, we shall suppress
1
from the notation, and simply write
\Gamma_{K}=\Gamma_{K,1}
and
\Gamma_{K}^{\text{\rm ad}}=\Gamma_{K,1}^{\text{\rm ad}}
.

By
[
Bor63
, Thm. 5.1]
,
\#(\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})/K)<\infty
; i.e., there exists a finite set of representatives
\{h_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that

(3.2.8)

\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})=\coprod_{i\in I}(\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})h_{i}K).

By substituting this into (
3.2.2
), and by using (
3.2.5
), we obtain disjoint unions

(3.2.9)

\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}=\coprod_{i\in I}\mathrm{S}_{K,h_{i}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\coprod_{i\in I}\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K,h_{i}}}^{\text{\rm an}},

which show that
\{\mathrm{S}_{K,h_{i}}^{\text{\rm an}}\}_{i\in I}
are the (finitely many) connected components of
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}
. The component labeled by
h=1
is called the
neutral component

(3.2.10)

\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\circ,\text{\rm an}}:=\mathrm{S}_{K,1}^{\text{\rm an}}=\Gamma_{K}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}=\Gamma_{K}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}.

When
K\subset\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
is
neat
as in
[
Pin89
, 0.6]
,
\Gamma_{K,h_{i}}\subset\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
and their images
\Gamma_{K,h_{i}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
are neat as in
[
Bor19
, 17.1 and 17.3]
. So
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K,h_{i}}}^{\text{\rm an}}\cong\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K,h_{i}}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm an}}
and
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}
are
complex manifolds
(viewed as smooth complex analytic spaces) as
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
is. For simplicity, we will only consider neat levels
K
’s, unless otherwise stated.

3.3.
Locally symmetric varieties and their compactifications

In this subsection, we shall simultaneously work with complex manifolds of the forms
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
,
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}
, and
\mathrm{S}_{K,h}^{\text{\rm an}}
introduced in Section
3.2
. To simplify the exposition, when the statements apply to all three kinds of manifolds above, we shall write
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
, where
\varGamma
can stand for either
\Gamma
, or
K
, or a pair of
K
and
h
. Later, when referring to results stated this way, we shall freely replace
\varGamma
with
\Gamma
etc depending on the context.

By
[
BB66
]
(applied to all connected components), the complex analytic space
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
is Zariski open dense in its so-called
Satake–Baily–Borel
or
minimal compactification

\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}
, which is the analytification of a normal projective variety
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
(over
\mathbb{C}
). Hence,
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
is the analytification of a (necessarily smooth) quasi-projective variety
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
. By
[
Bor72
, Thm. 3.7 and its proof]
and GAGA
[
Ser56
]
, any holomorphic map from the analytification of an algebraic variety over
\mathbb{C}
to
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
or
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}
uniquely algebraizes, and it follows that the algebraic structures of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
and
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
are unique. Varieties over
\mathbb{C}
of the form
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
are called
locally symmetric varieties
(although the notion is often reserved for the special case where
\varGamma=\Gamma
).

When
h=1
, we have the neutral components
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\circ}:=\mathrm{S}_{K,1}
,
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\circ,{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}:=\mathrm{S}_{K,1}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}
, and
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\circ,{\text{\rm min}}}:=\mathrm{S}_{K,1}^{\text{\rm min}}
. Therefore, the disjoint union (
3.2.9
) is the analytification of

(3.3.1)

\mathrm{S}_{K}=\mathrm{S}_{K}(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})=\coprod_{i\in I}\mathrm{S}_{K,h_{i}}=\coprod_{i\in I}\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K,h_{i}}}

(over
\mathbb{C}
), and its minimal compactification

(3.3.2)

\mathrm{S}_{K}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}:=\mathrm{S}_{K}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})=\coprod_{i\in I}\mathrm{S}_{K,h_{i}}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}

is the analytification of the canonical normal projective variety

(3.3.3)

\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm min}}:=\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm min}}(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})=\coprod_{i\in I}\mathrm{S}_{K,h_{i}}^{\text{\rm min}}

Remark 3.3.4
.

When
\mathrm{H}
is simply-connected (and semisimple),
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})
is connected in the real topology, by
[
PR94
, Sec. 7.2, Prop. 7.6]
, in which case
\mathsf{Y}=\mathsf{Y}^{+}
is connected,
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})_{+}=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{R})
, and
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
. Moreover, by strong approximation (see
[
PR94
, Sec. 7.4, Thm. 7.12]
),
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})K
, for any open compact subgroup
K
of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. Thus, in (
3.2.8
), the index set
I
is a singleton and can be chosen to be
\{1\}
. Therefore,
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\circ,\text{\rm an}}=\Gamma_{K}\backslash\mathsf{Y}
, where
\Gamma_{K}=\Gamma_{K,1}
, is connected as a complex manifold. This forces
\mathrm{S}_{K}
to be connected as an algebraic variety (over
\mathbb{C}
). In this case, we have
\mathrm{S}_{K}=\mathrm{S}_{K,h}=\mathrm{S}_{K,1}=\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\circ}
, for all
h\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
.

By
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III, Thm. 5.2, and Ch. IV, Thm. 2.2]
(again, applied to all connected components), there is a collection
\{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{\Sigma}
of normal projective varieties called
toroidal compactifications
—indexed by certain combinatorial data
\Sigma
called (collections of)
cone decompositions
, and we shall assume that all
\Sigma
’s are
projective
as in
[
AMRT10
, Ch. IV, Def. 2.1]
—such that the canonical open immersion
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
with dense image factors through
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
, where the first morphism is a canonical open immersion with dense image, whose (reduced closed) complement
\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}:=(\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}-\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma})_{\text{\rm red}}
is an effective divisor; and where the second morphism is proper (and necessarily surjective, by density of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
in both its source and target). The analytifications of all of these are denoted similarly, with additional superscripts “
an
”. When
\varGamma
is an open compact subgroup
K\subset\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, any
\Sigma
as above is a collection
\Sigma=\{\Sigma_{h}\}_{h\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})}
, where each
\Sigma_{h}
is a cone decomposition for the component
\mathrm{S}_{K,h}
. Without explaining the meaning of cone decompositions, let us still summarize the following qualitative facts:

Proposition 3.3.5
.

(1)

For each
\Sigma
, the above
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
and hence its algebraization
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
are uniquely characterized by the extension property defined by
\Sigma
as in
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III, Thm. 7.5]
.

(2)

Let
\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
