be the canonical morphism, which is an isomorphism over the open dense
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
. Hence, it induces
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma,*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}})
, by the normality of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
and Zariski’s main theorem.

(3)

\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
is locally isomorphic to analytifications of affine toroidal embeddings as in
[
KKMSD73
, Ch. I]
, and
\Sigma
tells us which affine toroidal embeddings are needed in such local descriptions. Concretely, when we work componentwise with some
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{\text{\rm an}}=\Gamma\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}\cong\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}

(
where, by abuse of notation,
\Sigma
means the cone decomposition for this particular component
)
, based on
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III]
, we have the following:

(a)

Let
\mathsf{F}
be a rational boundary component of
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
, which is a subset of
\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}}(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{C})/\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}(\mathbb{C})

(
see
(
3.1.6
))
stabilized by a
rational parabolic subgroup
of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, by
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III, Prop. 3.6]
. By
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III, Def. 3.12]
, this
(
real Lie
)
subgroup is of the form
\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, for a uniquely associated parabolic subgroup
\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
of the algebraic group
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}
over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
\mathrm{W}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}

(
resp.
\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
, resp.
\mathrm{V}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
)
denote the unipotent radical of
\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}

(
resp. center of
\mathrm{W}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
, resp.
\mathrm{W}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}/\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
)
.

(b)

Consider
\mathsf{Y}^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F}):=\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{C})\cdot\mathsf{Y}^{+}
in
\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}}(\mathbb{C})
, and
\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}:=\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})/\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{C})
. Then
\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\to\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}
is canonically a
\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{C})
-torsor, and
\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}\to\mathsf{F}
is canonically a
\mathrm{V}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})
-torsor.
(
See the summary near the end of
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III, Sec. 4.3]
.
)

(c)

Consider
\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}:=\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}\cap\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}:=\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}\cap\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
.

(d)

Consider
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}:=\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{C})/\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
, which is the analytification of an algebraic torus
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}
over
\mathbb{C}
with cocharacter group
\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
. Then
\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\to\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}
is canonically a
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}
-torsor. We shall denote
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}
by
{}^{\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}
when we want to emphasize its dependence on
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
.

(e)

Consider the algebraic toroidal embedding
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},{\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}}
, where
\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}
is given by
\Sigma
and admits an action of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}

(
with a finite number of orbits
)
. Then the pushout of the
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}
-torsor
\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\to\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}
via the analytification
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}
gives an analytic toroidal embedding
\bigl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\bigr)\hookrightarrow\bigl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\bigr)\times^{\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}
over
\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}
.

(f)

Finally, form the quotient of
\bigl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\bigr)\times^{\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}\to\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})^{\prime}\to\mathsf{F}
by
\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}/\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
. Then
\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{F}
gives a boundary stratum of
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}
, and an open subspace of
\bigl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}/\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\bigr)\backslash\bigl(\bigl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}^{+}(\mathsf{F})\bigr)\times^{\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm an}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}\bigr)
is what forms the an
open neighborhood
in
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
of the preimage of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{F}
via the canonical map
\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}^{\text{\rm an}}:\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{{\text{\rm min}},\text{\rm an}}
.

By passing to formal completions of both
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
and
\partial\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, which we can compare with their analytic analogues, and by Artin’s approximation
(
see
[
Art69
, Thm. 1.12, and the proof of the corollaries in Sec. 2]
)
, we see that
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is étale locally products of toroidal embeddings of the form
\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}
with identity morphisms of smooth schemes.

(4)

There is a condition on
\Sigma
, depending only on the subgroups
\Gamma_{\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
of
\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
as in
(
3c
)
, that ensures that all relevant toroidal embeddings above have smooth targets, in which case the underlying scheme of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is also smooth; and that
\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is a
normal crossings divisor
.

(5)

The collection
\Sigma
is partially ordered by
refinements
. For any finite number of given collections of cone decompositions
\Sigma_{i}
’s, we can find a
\Sigma
refining all of the
\Sigma_{i}
’s and satisfying any subset of the above conditions.

(6)

By
(
3
)
and
[
Ill02
, 7.3, (b)–(d)]
, if we equip
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
with the fs log structure induced by the divisor
\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, or equivalently the direct image of the trivial log structure on the open subvariety
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
, then
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is
log smooth
over
\mathbb{C}
. Then the sheaf of log differentials
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,1}

(
with the base field
\mathbb{C}
omitted from notation
)
, which is denoted by
\omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}/\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})}^{1}
in
[
Kat89
, (1.7)]
, and its exterior powers
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet}=\wedge^{\bullet}\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,1}
, are defined. In the top degree
d:=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathsf{Y}^{+})
, we have the
log canonical bundle

\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d}
. When
\Sigma
is smooth and
\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is a normal crossings divisor, we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,1}\cong\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{1}(\log\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}})
, the sheaf of differentials with log poles along
\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. In this case,
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d}
is the usual log canonical bundle.
(
This justifies the above terminologies.
)

(7)

Suppose
\Sigma
is smooth. By
[
Mum77
, Prop. 3.4]

(
and GAGA
[
Ser56
]
)
,
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet}
are the
canonical extensions

(
constructed for more general automorphic vector bundles in
[
Mum77
]
)
of
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}}^{\bullet}
, and
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d}
descends to an ample line bundle on
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
, which we shall abusively denote by
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
and call the
canonical bundle
on
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
. Thus,
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d}\cong\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}^{*}\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
, and so
(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d})^{\otimes m}\cong\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma,*}\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}^{*}\bigl((\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d})^{\otimes m}\bigr)\cong\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma,*}\bigl((\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d})^{\otimes m}\bigr)
, by
(
2
)
and the projection formula, for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Since
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
is ample over the proper scheme
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, we obtain
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}\cong\operatorname{Proj}\bigl(\oplus_{m\geq 0}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}},(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d})^{\otimes m})\bigr)\cong\operatorname{Proj}\bigl(\oplus_{m\geq 0}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}},(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d})^{\otimes m})\bigr)
.

(8)

The formation of minimal and toroidal compactifications is naturally compatible with direct products of the data involved.

3.4.
Compatibility with central isogenies

Lemma 3.4.1
.

Let
\varrho:\mathrm{H}_{1}\to\mathrm{H}_{2}
be any central isogeny of connected algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
. Then it induces an isomorphism
\varrho^{\text{\rm ad}}:\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{H}_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}
. Equivalently,
Z(\mathrm{H}_{1})
is the schematic preimage of
Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})
; i.e.,
Z(\mathrm{H}_{1})\cong Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})\times_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}\mathrm{H}_{1}
.

Proof.

This is well known, but let us still include some explanation, for the convenience of the readers. Since the base field
\mathbb{Q}
is of characteristic zero,
\varrho
is finite étale. Therefore, we may verify the lemma by base change from
\mathbb{Q}
to any algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
of
\mathbb{Q}
, and compare the points of
Z(\mathrm{H}_{1})
and
Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})\times_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}\mathrm{H}_{1}
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Since
\varrho
is a central isogeny between connected algebraic groups, it is surjective and induces a surjection homomorphism
\varrho(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}):\mathrm{H}_{1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathrm{H}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, which maps
Z(\mathrm{H}_{1})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
to
Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Conversely, suppose
h_{0}\in(Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})\times_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}\mathrm{H}_{1})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, or equivalently a point of
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
mapped to
Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
in
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Consider the commutator morphism
\mathrm{H}_{1,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to\mathrm{H}_{1,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}:h\mapsto h_{0}hh_{0}^{-1}h^{-1}
. Since
h_{0}
is mapped to
Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
in
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, the morphism factors through the discrete
\ker(\varrho({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))
, or rather the identity, by the connectedness of
\mathrm{H}_{1}
(and hence of
\mathrm{H}_{1,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
). Thus,
h_{0}\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, as desired.
∎

Let
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
denote the derived group of
\mathrm{H}
(see
[
CGP10
, Def. A.1.14]
and the references there). Then
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
is connected and semisimple. By
[
CGP10
, Cor. A.4.11]
, there is a central isogeny
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
from a simply-connected connected semisimple algebraic group
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}
, which we shall call the
simply-connected cover
of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
, whose formation is compatible with base change from
\mathbb{Q}
to any extension field. Let
\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm sc}},{\text{\rm ad}}}:=\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}/Z(\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}})
and
\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},{\text{\rm ad}}}:=\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}/Z(\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}})
, as in the case of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}=\mathrm{H}/Z(\mathrm{H})
.

Lemma 3.4.2
.

(1)

The canonical homomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm sc}},{\text{\rm ad}}}\to\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},{\text{\rm ad}}}
induced by
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
is an isomorphism.

(2)

The canonical homomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},{\text{\rm ad}}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}
induced by the composition of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{H}\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is an isomorphism. Equivalently,
Z(\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}})=Z(\mathrm{H})\cap\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
in
\mathrm{H}
; i.e.,
Z(\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}})\cong Z(\mathrm{H})\times_{\mathrm{H}}\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
.

Proof.

Again, these are well known, but let us still include some explanations, for the convenience of the readers. As for (
1
), it is a special case of Lemma
3.4.1
. As for (
2
), since
H^{\text{\rm der}}
is a subgroup of
\mathrm{H}
, we have
Z(\mathrm{H})\cap\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}\subset Z(\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}})
, essentially by definition. Conversely, since
\mathrm{H}
is reductive, the homomorphism
Z(\mathrm{H})\times\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{H}
of algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
is surjective, by comparing Lie algebras. Therefore,
\mathrm{H}=Z(\mathrm{H})\cdot\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
, and we obtain
Z(\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}})\subset Z(\mathrm{H})\cap\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
, as desired.
∎

In the following constructions, let us assume that the following holds:

Condition 3.4.3
.

Suppose we have pairs
(\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})
and
(\mathrm{H}_{2},\mathsf{Y}_{2})
as in Section
3.1
, together with a group homomorphism
\varrho:\mathrm{H}_{1}\to\mathrm{H}_{2}
inducing a central isogeny
\varrho^{\text{\rm der}}:\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{H}_{2}^{\text{\rm der}}
and, by Lemma
3.4.1
, also an isomorphism
\varrho^{\text{\rm ad}}:\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{H}_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}
. Suppose we also have a holomorphic map
\varrho_{\mathsf{Y}}:\mathsf{Y}_{1}\to\mathsf{Y}_{2}
equivariant with
\varrho^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R}):\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{H}_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{R})
, which necessarily induces an isomorphism from any fixed choice of a connected component
\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}
of
\mathsf{Y}_{1}
to a connected component
\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+}
of
\mathsf{Y}_{2}
.

Construction 3.4.4
.

Let
\Gamma_{1}\subset\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and
\Gamma_{2}\subset\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
be neat arithmetic subgroups of
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, respectively, such that
\varrho^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})(\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}})\subset\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}
in
\mathrm{H}_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
. Since
\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+}
, the canonical morphism
[1]_{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}:\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\Gamma_{1}\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}=\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\Gamma_{1}\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+}=\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+}
is finite étale surjective, which uniquely algebraizes to a finite étale surjective morphism
[1]_{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}
, by
[
Bor72
]
and GAGA
[
Ser56
]
.

Construction 3.4.5
.

In Construction
3.4.4
, suppose moreover that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are projective cone decompositions for
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}
, respectively, such that
\Sigma_{1}
refines the pullback of
\Sigma_{2}
. By comparing the extension properties of
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
and
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
as in
[
AMRT10
, Ch. III, Thm. 7.5]
and by GAGA
[
Ser56
]
, for
?=\emptyset
and
an
, the morphism
[1]_{\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}}^{?}

(
uniquely
)
extends to a proper surjective morphism
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2}\Sigma_{2})}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}:\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}
such that
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}^{?}
is the open preimage of
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{?}
.

Construction 3.4.6
.

Let
g\in\mathrm{H}_{2}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. Let
K_{1}\subset\mathrm{H}_{1}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
and
K_{2}\subset\mathrm{H}_{2}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
be neat open compact subgroups such that
\varrho({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})(K_{1})\subset gK_{2}g^{-1}
. Then we have a canonical morphism
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}^{\text{\rm an}}(\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}(\mathrm{H}_{2},\mathsf{Y}_{2})
defined by right multiplication by
g
, which uniquely algebraize to a canonical morphism
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}(\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}(\mathrm{H}_{2},\mathsf{Y}_{2})
, by
[
Bor72
]
and GAGA
[
Ser56
]
. If
\varrho
is an isomorphism, then
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}
and
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}
are surjective.

Construction 3.4.7
.

In Construction
3.4.6
, suppose
\varrho({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
maps
h_{1}\in\mathrm{H}_{1}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
to
h_{2}\in\mathrm{H}_{2}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. For
?=\emptyset
and
an
, the morphism
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{?}
there induces a finite étale surjective morphism
[g]_{(K_{1},h_{1}),(K_{2},h_{2}g)}^{?}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1}}^{?}\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}g}^{?}
, which can be identified with the morphism
[1]_{\Gamma_{K_{1},h_{1}},\Gamma_{K_{2},h_{2}g}}^{?}:\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K_{1},h_{1}}}^{?}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K_{2},h_{2}g}}^{?}
in Construction
3.4.4
. Consequently, for
?=\emptyset
and
an
, the morphism
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{?}
in Construction
3.4.6
is also finite étale, because it is a disjoint union of morphisms
[g]_{(K_{1},h_{1}),(K_{2},h_{2}g)}^{?}
here.

Construction 3.4.8
.

In Construction
3.4.7
, suppose moreover that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are cone decompositions for
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1}}
and
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}g}
, respectively, such that
\Sigma_{1}
refines the pullback of
\Sigma_{2}
via
[g]_{(K_{1},h_{1}),(K_{2},h_{2}g)}
. By Construction
3.4.5
, with
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{K_{1},h_{1}}
and
\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{K_{2},h_{2}g}
, we see that, for
?=\emptyset
and
an
, the morphism
[g]_{(K_{1},h_{1}),(K_{2},h_{2}g)}^{?}
extends to a proper surjective morphism
[g]_{(K_{1},h_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(K_{2},h_{2}g,\Sigma_{2})}^{?}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}g,\Sigma_{2}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}
such that
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1}}^{?}
is the open preimage of
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}g}^{?}
.

Construction 3.4.9
.

In Construction
3.4.6
, suppose moreover that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are cone decompositions for
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}
and
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}
, respectively, such that
K_{1}
refines the pullback of
K_{2}
in the sense that
\Sigma_{1,h_{1}}
refines the pullback of
\Sigma_{2,h_{2}g}
as in Construction
3.4.8
, for each

mapped to

via

. By applying Construction
3.4.8
to all
h_{1}\in\mathrm{H}_{1}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, we see that, for
?=\emptyset
and
an
, the finite morphism
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{?}
in Construction
3.4.6
extends to a proper morphism
[g]_{(K_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(K_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{?}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},?}
such that
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}^{?}
is the open preimage of
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}^{?}
. For
?=\emptyset
and
an
, when
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{?}
is surjective, so is
[g]_{(K_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(K_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{?}
.

Construction 3.4.10
.

Let
(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})
be any pair as in Section
3.1
. Consider the tower
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}\}_{K}
, indexed by neat open compact subgroups
K
of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. For
g\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
and open compact subgroups
K_{1}
and

of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
such that
K_{1}\subset gK_{2}g^{-1}
, right multiplication by
g
on the second factor of

induces

, which uniquely algebraizes to

, as in Construction
3.4.6

(
with

in Condition

)
. Since
\varrho_{\mathsf{Y}}=\operatorname{Id}_{\mathsf{Y}}
, all morphisms
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}
and hence

as above are finite étale surjective, which compatibly define canonical right
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
-actions on
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}\}_{K}
, for
?=\emptyset
and
an
.

Construction 3.4.11
.

In Construction
3.4.10
, for each
g\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
normalizing
(
resp. contained in
)
a particular
K
, we have
hKg=hgK

(
resp.
hKg=hK
)
, and hence right multiplication on
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
defines an action
(
resp. trivial action
)
of
K
on the double coset space
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\big\backslash\bigl(\mathsf{Y}^{+}\times\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})\bigr)\big/K
which permutes
(
resp. fixes
)
its connected components
\mathrm{S}_{K,h}^{\text{\rm an}}
. Therefore, when

is a normal subgroup of
K
, for
?=\emptyset
and

, the action of
K
on
[1]_{K^{\prime},K}^{?}:\mathrm{S}_{K^{\prime}}^{?}\to\mathrm{S}_{K}^{?}
factors through
K/K^{\prime}
, fixes the target
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{?}
, and induces actions of
K/K^{\prime}
on the fibers.

Proposition 3.4.12
.

In the setting of Construction
3.4.11
, let us denote closures of subgroups of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
by overlines. Note that
K\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))=K\cdot(\overline{Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})})
and
\overline{K\cap(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))}=K\cap\overline{(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))}
, for any open compact subgroup
K
of
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})
. Suppose
K^{\prime}\subset K
are neat open compact subgroups of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. Then, for
?=\emptyset
and
an
, the actions of
K/K^{\prime}
in Construction
3.4.11
factor through

(3.4.13)

K/\bigl(K^{\prime}\cdot(K\cap\overline{(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))})\bigr)\cong\bigl(K\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)\big/\bigl(K^{\prime}\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)

and makes
[1]_{K^{\prime},K}^{?}:\mathrm{S}_{K^{\prime}}^{?}\to\mathrm{S}_{K}^{?}
an étale torsor under this finite group
(
3.4.13
)
.

Proof.

Since
[1]_{K^{\prime},K}
is finite étale, it suffices to prove this proposition in the analytic case. Let us study the fiber of
[1]_{K^{\prime},K}^{\text{\rm an}}:\mathrm{S}_{K^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm an}}\to\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}
over a double coset
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}(y,h)K
in
\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm an}}
, where
y\in\mathsf{Y}^{+}
and
h\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. By definition, any member of this fiber is a double coset
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})K^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{S}_{K^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm an}}
, where
y^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Y}^{+}
and
h^{\prime}\in\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, such that
y^{\prime}=\gamma y
and
h^{\prime}=\gamma hk
for some
\gamma\in\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and
k\in K
. Up to replacing
(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})
with
(\gamma^{-1}y^{\prime},\gamma^{-1}h^{\prime})
, we may assume that
y^{\prime}=y
and
h^{\prime}\in hK
, without changing
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})K^{\prime}
. In particular,
K/K^{\prime}
acts transitively on the fiber. Since every
\gamma^{\prime}\in Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})
satisfies
(y,h^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma^{\prime}y,\gamma^{\prime}h^{\prime})=\gamma^{\prime}(y,h^{\prime})
, right multiplication by
\gamma^{\prime}
acts trivially on the fiber. Hence, the action of
K/K^{\prime}
factors through (
3.4.13
). If
k^{\prime},k^{\prime\prime}\in K
and
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}(y,hk^{\prime})K^{\prime}=\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}(y,hk^{\prime\prime})K^{\prime}
, then
y=\gamma^{\prime}y
and
hk^{\prime}=\gamma^{\prime}hk^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime\prime\prime}
, for some
\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and
k^{\prime\prime\prime}\in K^{\prime}
. In this case,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap(hKh^{-1})=\Gamma_{K,h}
. Since
K
is neat,
\Gamma_{K,h}^{\text{\rm ad}}
acts freely on
\mathsf{Y}
. Since
\gamma^{\prime}
fixes
y
, its image in
\Gamma_{K,h}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is trivial, and hence
\gamma^{\prime}\in\ker(\Gamma_{K,h}\to\Gamma_{K,h}^{\text{\rm ad}})\subset Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})
. Since
\gamma^{\prime}\in hKh^{-1}
, we have
\gamma^{\prime}=h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}h\in K
. Thus,
k^{\prime}=h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}hk^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime\prime\prime}=k^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime\prime\prime}\gamma^{\prime}\in k^{\prime\prime}K^{\prime}(K\cap\overline{(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))})
, and (
3.4.13
) acts faithfully (and transitively) on the fiber, as desired.
∎

Proposition 3.4.14
.

Suppose Condition
3.4.3
holds, so that we have the constructions following it. Suppose that
(g,\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2})
is one of the following:

(1)

(1,\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
for some
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
as in Construction
3.4.4
;

(2)

some
(g,K_{1},K_{2})
as in Construction
3.4.6
;

(3)

some
(g,(K_{1},h_{1}),(K_{2},h_{2}g))
as in Construction
3.4.7
.

We shall refer to these as Cases
1
,
2
, and
3
, respectively. Then:

(1)

The canonical finite étale morphism
[g]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}
of smooth quasi-projective varieties is surjective in Cases
1
and
3
; and, when
\varrho
is an isomorphism, also in Case
2
.

(2)

The morphism
[g]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}
extends to a canonical finite morphism

(3.4.15)

[g]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}

of normal projective varieties, which is surjective when
[g]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}
is. Hence,
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}
is the normalization of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}
via
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}
, by Zariski’s main theorem.

(3)

If
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are as in Constructions
3.4.5
,
3.4.8
, and
3.4.9
, then the canonical proper morphism
[g]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is a
log étale
morphism of fs log schemes whose underlying schemes are normal projective varieties, which is surjective when
[g]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}
is. Hence,
[g]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{{\text{\rm tor}},*}\,\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet}\cong\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet}
. By the arguments in
[
KKMSD73
, Ch. I, Sec. 3, especially p. 44, Cor. 2]
, by the local freeness of
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet}
in all degrees, and by the projection formula, it follows that the canonical
(
adjunction
)
morphism

(3.4.16)

(\Omega_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}^{\log,\bullet})^{\otimes m}\to R[g]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}),*}^{\text{\rm tor}}\bigl((\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet})^{\otimes m}\bigr)

is an isomorphism
(
resp. the zero morphism
)
over the connected components of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
that are in
(
resp. not in
)
the image of
[g]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}
, for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}

(
with negative tensor powers of
\Omega_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,\bullet}}
defined by duality
)
.

(4)

If the above
\Sigma_{1}
is exactly the pullback of
\Sigma_{2}
, then
[g]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is
finite Kummer étale
, and
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is the normalization of
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
via
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, by Zariski’s main theorem again.
(
The assumption makes sense because the pullback
\Sigma_{1}
of a general projective
\Sigma_{2}
is projective. On the contrary, the pullback
\Sigma_{1}
of a general smooth
\Sigma_{2}
is not necessarily smooth.
)

(5)

Suppose that one of the following holds:

(A
′
)

(g,\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2})=(1,\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
is as in Case
1
, and
\varrho^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})(\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}})
is a normal subgroup of
\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}
, in which case
\overline{\varGamma}:=\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}\big/\bigl(\varrho^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})(\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}})\bigr)
;

(B
′
)

(g,\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2})=(1,K_{1},K_{2})
is as in Case
2
,
\varrho
is an isomorphism, and
\varrho({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})(K_{1})
is a normal subgroup of
K_{2}
, in which case
\overline{\varGamma}:=K_{2}/\bigl(\varrho({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})(K_{1})\cdot(K_{2}\cap\overline{(Z(\mathrm{H}_{2})(\mathbb{Q}))})\bigr)

(
cf.
(
3.4.13
))
.

In both cases,
\overline{\varGamma}
is a finite group. Then:

(a)

The natural action of
\varGamma_{2}
on
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}
factors through
\overline{\varGamma}
and makes the finite étale surjective morphism
[1]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}
an étale torsor under
\overline{\varGamma}
. In this case,
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}/\overline{\varGamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}
.

(b)

The action of
\overline{\varGamma}
on
[1]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}

(
necessarily uniquely
)
extends to the finite surjective morphism
[1]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}
in
(
3.4.15
)
, and induces
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}/\overline{\varGamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}
.

(c)

If
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are as in
(
4
)
, then the action of
\overline{\varGamma}
on
[1]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}

(
necessarily uniquely
)
extends to the finite Kummer étale surjective morphism
[1]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, and makes the latter a Kummer étale torsor under
\overline{\varGamma}
. In this case,
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}}/\overline{\varGamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. The actions of
\overline{\varGamma}
on
[1]_{(\varGamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\varGamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
and
[1]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}
are compatible.

Proof.

Firstly, (
1
) follows from Constructions
3.4.4
,
3.4.6
, and
3.4.7
. In the latter two constructions, if
\varrho({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})(h_{1})=h_{2}
, then
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}^{\text{\rm an}}\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}^{\text{\rm an}}
maps the connected component
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1}}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K_{1},h_{1}}}^{\text{\rm an}}\cong\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K_{1},h_{1}}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm an}}
of
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}^{\text{\rm an}}
to
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}g}^{\text{\rm an}}=\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K_{2},h_{2}g}}^{\text{\rm an}}\cong\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{K_{2},h_{2}g}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm an}}
. Hence, it suffices to prove (
2
)–(
4
) in Case
1
.

If
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are as in Construction
3.4.5
, then
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}:\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
is locally given by
{}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\prime}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}\to{}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}
, in the notation of Proposition
3.3.5
(
3
), where
\{\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\prime}\}
is a refinement of
\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}
, for the same rational boundary component
\mathsf{F}
of
\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+}
. Note that
{}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}\to{}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F}}
is an isogeny of tori, because the corresponding homomorphism of cocharacter groups
\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}\cap\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})\to\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}\cap\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
is between two arithmetic subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
. By passing to formal completions and by Artin’s approximation as in Proposition
3.3.5
(
3
),
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is étale locally of the form
{}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\prime}\}}\to{}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}
, which is étale locally some standard log étale morphisms as in
[
Kat89
, Prop. 3.4]
. Hence,
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is log étale as a morphism of fs log schemes. The remaining statements in (
3
) then follow.

Up to refining both
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
, suppose moreover that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are not only projective but also smooth. Since
\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+}
, we have
d=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathsf{Y}_{2}^{+})
. By Proposition
3.3.5
(
7
), we obtain a canonical morphism

(3.4.17)

\begin{split}\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}&\cong\operatorname{Proj}\bigl(\oplus_{m\geq 0}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}},(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d})^{\otimes m})\bigr)\\
\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}&\cong\operatorname{Proj}\bigl(\oplus_{m\geq 0}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}},(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d})^{\otimes m})\bigr)\end{split}

which is necessarily proper and pulls
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
back to
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
. Since the ample line bundle
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
on
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{\text{\rm min}}
pulls back to the ample line bundle
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d}
on
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}^{\text{\rm min}}
, this proper morphism (
3.4.15
) is quasi-affine and hence finite. Since any finite number of cone decompositions admit a common projective smooth refinement, it follows from (
3.4.16
) that the morphism (
3.4.17
) is independent of the choices of
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
. Thus, (
3.4.17
) gives the desired finite morphism (
3.4.15
), and the remaining statements in (
2
) are self-explanatory.

Again assuming that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are projective but not necessarily smooth, if
\Sigma_{1}
is exactly the pullback of
\Sigma_{2}
, then
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is locally given by
{}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}\to{}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}^{\text{\rm an}}
, as before, but with the
same

\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}
on both sides. In this case, since
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
has finite fibers, the proper morphism
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is quasi-finite and hence
finite
. By Artin’s approximation for pullbacks of the finite morphism to formal completions on the target,
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is étale locally on the target of the form
{}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}\to{}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\text{\rm ad}}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathsf{F},\{\sigma_{\alpha}\}}
, which is étale locally on the target some standard log étale morphisms as in
[
Kat89
, Prop. 3.4]
associated with Kummer homomorphisms of monoids as in
[
Ill02
, 1.4]
. Thus,
[1]_{(\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1}),(\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is finite Kummer étale as a morphism of fs log schemes, and the remaining statements in (
4
) follow.

Finally, in the setting of (
5
), the statements in (
5a
) are self-explanatory in Case
5
, and follow from Proposition
3.4.12
in Case
5
. As for (
5b
) and (
5c
), the action of
\overline{\varGamma}
extends to the minimal and toroidal compactifications and have the desired properties by taking normalizations and resorting to Zariski’s main theorem, as in the last sentences of (
2
) and (
4
). The extended actions of
\overline{\varGamma}
to the compactifications are necessarily unique and compatible with each other, because they are determined by their restrictions to
[1]_{\varGamma_{1},\varGamma_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma_{2}}
by density.
∎

Remark 3.4.18
.

A useful special case of Case
2
of Proposition
3.4.14
is when
\mathrm{H}_{1}
is semisimple and simply-connected, so that
\mathrm{H}_{1}=\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm der}}=\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm sc}}
. Then
\mathsf{Y}_{1}=\mathsf{Y}_{1}^{+}
,
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{R})_{+}=\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
,
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}=\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
, and
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1}}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}^{\circ}
, for all
h_{1}\in\mathrm{H}_{1}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, as explained in Remark
3.3.4
. In this case, we can study
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}
and its compactifications as in Section
3.3
, and deduce results for
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}}
for all
h_{2}\in\mathrm{H}_{2}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, including
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}^{\circ}=\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},1}
, and their compactifications.

Remark 3.4.19
.

In Case
2
of Proposition
3.4.14
, if we assume that
\mathrm{H}_{1}
is semisimple and simply-connected, as in Remark
3.4.18
; that the central isogeny
\varrho^{\text{\rm der}}:\mathrm{H}_{1}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{H}_{2}^{\text{\rm der}}
is an
isomorphism
; and that
K_{1}=\varrho({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})^{-1}(gK_{2}g^{-1})
. Then we obtain an isomorphism
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1},h_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2},h_{2}g}
of connected components, which is the restriction of the open-and-closed immersion
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}:\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}
from the connected
\mathrm{S}_{K_{1}}
into the whole (generally disconnected)
\mathrm{S}_{K_{2}}
.

By Case
2
in Proposition
3.4.14
, in the setting in Construction
3.4.10
, we have:

Corollary 3.4.20
.

The tower
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}\}_{K}
in Construction
3.4.10
, with its canonical
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
-action given by finite étale surjective morphisms as in Proposition
3.4.14
(
1
)
, admits a tower of minimal compactifications
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm min}}\}_{K}
, which are normal projective varieties, with a canonical right
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
-action extending that on
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}\}_{K}
, given by finite surjective morphisms as in Proposition
3.4.14
(
2
)
. It also admits a double tower of toroidal compactifications
\{\mathrm{S}_{K,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{(K,\Sigma)}
, which are projective normal varieties, with a canonical right
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
-action compatible with those on
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}\}_{K}
and
\{\mathrm{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm min}}\}_{K}
, given by proper log étale morphisms
(
resp. finite Kummer étale
)
as in Proposition
3.4.14
(
3
)

(
resp.
(
4
))
, when cone decompositions on the sources refine
(
resp. are exactly
)
the pullbacks of those on the targets.

Corollary 3.4.21
.

In the setting of Corollary
3.4.20
, given any neat open compact subgroup
K
of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, and any normal subgroup
K_{0}
of
K
, the canonical action of
K
on the tower
\{\mathrm{S}_{K^{\prime}}\}_{K_{0}\subset K^{\prime}\subset K}

(
resp.
\{\mathrm{S}_{K^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{0}\subset K^{\prime}\subset K}
)
indexed by open subgroups
K^{\prime}
of
K
containing
K_{0}

(
where
\Sigma^{\prime}
abusively denotes the pullback of
\Sigma
for each level
K^{\prime}
)
factors through a faithful action of the following quotient group

(3.4.22)

K/\bigl(\overline{K_{0}\cdot\bigl(K\cap(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)}\bigr)\cong\bigl(K\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)\big/\overline{\bigl(K_{0}\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)},

where overlines denote closures of subgroups of
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
as in Proposition
3.4.12
.

Proof.

Note that

\begin{split}\overline{K_{0}\cdot\bigl(K\cap(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)}&=\cap_{K_{0}\subset K^{\prime}\subset K}\overline{K^{\prime}\cdot\bigl(K\cap(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr)}\\
&=\cap_{K_{0}\subset K^{\prime}\subset K}K^{\prime}\cdot\bigl(K\cap\overline{(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))}\bigr)\end{split}

and

\overline{K_{0}\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))}=\cap_{K_{0}\subset K^{\prime}\subset K}\bigl(K^{\prime}\cdot(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))\bigr).

Then the assertions follow from Proposition
3.4.14
(
5
) and Corollary
3.4.20
.
∎

Example 3.4.23
.

Let
K^{p}\subset\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}})
and
K_{p}\subset\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be open compact subgroups. In Corollary
3.4.21
, let
K_{0}=K^{p}
and
K=K^{p}K_{p}
. Then
(
3.4.22
)
becomes

(3.4.24)

(K^{p}K_{p})/\bigl(K^{p}\cdot\bigl((K^{p}K_{p})\cap\overline{(Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q}))}\bigr)\bigr).

This is consistent with
[
Del79
, 2.1.9]
in the setting of Shimura varieties
(
which we shall consider later in Section
4.1
)
. In
[
RC22
, Sec. 4.1]
, this is denoted by
\widetilde{K}_{p}
, with
\mathrm{H}
and
Z(\mathrm{H})
here denoted by
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{Z}
there, respectively.

Remark 3.4.25
.

Since
Z(\mathrm{H})^{\circ}/Z_{s}(\mathrm{H})
has the same split ranks over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{R}
, the closure
\overline{Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})}
of
Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})
in
Z(\mathrm{H})({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
(and hence in
\mathrm{H}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
) is contained in
Z_{s}(\mathrm{H})({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
. Thus, we have surjections
K\twoheadrightarrow K/(K\cap(\overline{Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})}))\twoheadrightarrow K^{c}
, where
K/(K\cap(\overline{Z(\mathrm{H})(\mathbb{Q})}))
is as in (
3.4.22
) (with
K_{0}=\{1\}
in the notation there).

3.5.
Automorphic vector bundles and their canonical extensions

Let
(\mathrm{H},\mathsf{Y})
be any pair as in Section
3.1
, and let
\varGamma
be as in Section
3.3
, so that
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
and its minimal and toroidal compactifications are defined.

Recall that, by our convention,
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c})
and
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
denote the categories of finite-dimensional representations of
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c}
and
\mathrm{M}^{c}
, respectively, over
\mathbb{C}
. Each
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c})
defines a
\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}
-equivariant vector bundle on
\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}}
whose analytification pulls back via (
3.1.6
) to
\mathsf{Y}^{+}
, naturally descends to a vector bundle on any
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
we consider, and canonically algebraizes to a vector bundle on
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
, called the
automorphic vector bundle
associated with
W
. Moreover, by Remark
3.1.4
,
W
is equipped with a canonical decreasing filtration
\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}W
by subrepresentations of
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c}
. By abuse of notation, we shall denote by
\underline{W}
all of these vector bundles (including their analytifications) associated with
W
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c})
, and denote the filtrations on them by
\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}=\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}\,\underline{W}
. The assignment
W\mapsto(\underline{W},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
defines a tensor functor from
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c})
to the category of filtered vector bundles over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
. For each (projective) cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
\mathrm{S}_{K}
, the analytic
(\underline{W},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
on
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm an}}
admits a
canonical extension

(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{{\text{\rm tor}},\text{\rm an}}
, which algebraizes by GAGA
[
Ser56
]
to a vector bundle which we still denote by
(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, whose restriction to
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
is the above algebraic
(\underline{W},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
. (See
[
Har89
, Thm. 1.4.1 and 4.2]
for the above statements in the context of Shimura varieties, which we will introduce later in Section
4.1
. The arguments in the proofs there apply here, because they are over individual connected components and use nothing more than general properties of locally symmetric varieties.) We shall say that the algebraic
(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is the canonical extension of the algebraic
(\underline{W},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
. When the context is clear, we will often suppress
\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}
from the notation. For
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
, we shall view it as a representation of
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c}
via the canonical homomorphism
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c}\to\mathrm{M}^{c}
, and all the above still applies.

Remark 3.5.1
.

It follows immediately from the characterizing property of canonical extensions as in
[
Har89
, (4.1.1)]
that the formation of canonical extensions as above is compatible with all morphisms between toroidal compactifications in Proposition
3.4.14
(
3
). See
[
Har89
, 4.3.1, 4.3.2, and 4.3.3]
for some explicitly stated cases.

Remark 3.5.2
.

As in Proposition
3.3.5
(
7
) (based on
[
Mum77
, Prop. 3.4]
), for
W=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M})
(with adjoint action), we have
\underline{\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}}\cong\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}}^{1}
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
, which extends to
(\underline{\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}})^{\text{\rm can}}\cong\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,1}\cong\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{1}(\log\partial\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}})
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
when
\Sigma
is smooth.

For
V\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{G}^{c})
, the filtered vector bundle
(\underline{V}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
associated with the restriction
V|_{\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c}}
as above admits the additional structure of a canonical integrable log connection
\nabla:\underline{V}^{\text{\rm can}}\to\underline{V}^{\text{\rm can}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}}^{\log,1}
satisfying Griffiths transversality; i.e.,
\nabla(\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})\subset(\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet-1})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}}^{\log,1}
. By restriction to
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
, we obtain a canonical integral connection
\nabla
with regular singularity (still satisfying Griffiths transversality). In order to emphasize that such constructions are meant to be useful for studying the de Rham cohomology, we shall denote by
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
and
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
the associated filtered connections and log connections, respectively.

Remark 3.5.3
.

By
[
Har89
, (4.2.2)]
, the above
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla)
above is canonically isomorphic to the canonical extension of
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V},\nabla)
introduced by Deligne in
[
Del70
]
.

3.6.
Automorphic line bundles of positive parallel weights

Let us continue with the setting of Section
3.5
. Let
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}
be the simply-connected cover of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
, as in Section
3.3
. Let
\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},c}
abusively denote the image of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
in
\mathrm{H}^{c}
.

Construction 3.6.1
.

By Lemma
3.4.2
, we have central isogenies
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},c}\to\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}
. Let us abusively and temporarily denote
(
in this subsection
)
the induced central isogenies of Levi subgroups by
\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm sc}}\to\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{M}^{{\text{\rm der}},c}\to\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm ad}}
.

Construction 3.6.2
.

Since
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}
is connected, semisimple, and simply-connected, it decomposes into a product
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm sc}}\cong\prod_{j\in J}\mathrm{H}_{j}
of its
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factors. Accordingly,
\mathsf{Y}^{+}\cong\prod_{j\in J}\mathsf{Y}^{+}_{j}
, and each
(\mathrm{H}_{j},\mathsf{Y}^{+}_{j})
is still a pair as in Section
3.1
. By Lemma
3.4.2
, we have a compatible product
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}\cong\prod_{j\in J}\mathrm{H}_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}
of adjoint quotients.

Construction 3.6.3
.

For each
j\in J

(
as above
)
, denote by
\mathrm{M}_{j}
,
\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}}
, etc the respective pullbacks of the subgroups
\mathrm{M}
,
\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
, etc of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}
to
\mathrm{H}_{j,\mathbb{C}}
; and abusively denote by
\mathrm{M}_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}
the pullback of the
(
abusively denoted
)
subgroup
\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm ad}}
of
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}

(
as in Construction
3.6.1
)
to
\mathrm{H}_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}
. The adjoint action of
\mathrm{M}
on
\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
factors through
\mathrm{M}^{c}
and
\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm ad}}

(
because
\ker(\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{M}^{c})\subset\ker(\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm ad}})=Z(\mathrm{H})
by definition
)
and defines
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
and
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{\text{\rm ad}})
, which pulls back to
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}}
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}_{j})
and
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}})
, for each
j\in J
.
(
We have similar statements with “
std
” omitted.
)

Definition 3.6.4
.

We say an irreducible
W
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
or
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c})
is of
positive parallel weight
if, for each
j\in J
(as above), and for some (and equivalently every) choice of maximal torus
\mathrm{T}_{j}
of
\mathrm{M}_{j}
, one weight of the pullback
W_{j}
of
W
to
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}_{j})
(with respect to
\mathrm{T}_{j}
) is a positive multiple of the weight of
\det(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}})
.

Remark 3.6.5
.

By the theory of highest weights, the condition in Definition
3.6.4
forces
W_{j}
, for all
j\in J
, and hence
W
to be
one-dimensional
representations. In this case, there exist
a_{j},b_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
W_{j}^{\otimes a_{j}}\cong\det(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}})^{\otimes b_{j}}
, for all
j\in J
.

Proposition 3.6.6
.

Let
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
be irreducible of positive parallel weight, as in Definition
3.6.4
, which is necessarily one-dimensional, by Remark
3.6.5
. Let
\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
be as in Proposition
3.3.5
(
2
)
. Let
\underline{W}
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
and
\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}}
over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
be automorphic line bundles as in Section
3.5
.
(
We shall omit “
\otimes
” when denoting tensor powers of line bundles.
)
Then there exists
m_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}})^{m_{0}}\cong\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}^{*}L
for some
ample
line bundle
L
on
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
. In this case,
\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}}
is semiample, and there exists
(
by general theory
)
some
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
L^{r}
is very ample, so that
(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}})^{m}
is globally generated over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
for all positive multiples
m
of
rm_{0}
, and so that the induced morphism
\phi_{m}:\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathbb{P}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}},(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}})^{m})\cong\mathbb{P}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}},L^{\frac{m}{m_{0}}})
factors through
\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}
and the canonical closed immersion
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}H^{0}(\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}^{\text{\rm min}},L^{\frac{m}{m_{0}}})
. In particular,
\phi_{m}|_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}}
is an immersion as
\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}|_{\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}}
is, and
\underline{W}
is ample over
\mathrm{S}_{\varGamma}
.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
Lan16
, Lem. 3.2]
, up to some minor changes of notation and conventions. But let us spell out the details, for the sake of clarity.

By working over connected components, it suffices to prove Proposition
3.6.6
in the special case where
\varGamma=\Gamma
is a neat arithmetic subgroup of
\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
. By Proposition
3.4.14
(
3
), we may also replace
\Sigma
with any projective smooth refinement.

By Remark
3.6.5
, there exists
m_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
W_{j}^{\otimes m_{0}}\cong\det(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}})^{\otimes c_{j}}
, for some
c_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, which can be viewed as a representation in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}})
, for each
j\in J
. Let
\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}
denote the image of
\Gamma^{\text{\rm ad}}
in
\mathrm{H}_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
, and let
\Sigma_{j}
be any projective smooth cone decomposition for
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}=\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{j}^{+}
, for each
j\in J
. Consider
\overline{\Gamma}:=\prod_{j\in J}\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm ad}}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and the cone decomposition
\overline{\Sigma}:=\prod_{j\in J}\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}
for
\mathrm{S}_{\overline{\Gamma}}
. Let
\Sigma
be any (projective) refinement of the pullback of
\overline{\Sigma}
to
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}
. By Propositions
3.3.5
and
3.4.14
, and by Remark
3.5.2
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[1]_{(\Gamma,\Sigma),(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Sigma})}^{\text{\rm tor}}}
\scriptstyle{\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{S}_{\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Sigma}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\textstyle\oint_{\overline{\Sigma}}}
\textstyle{{\prod_{j\in J}\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}},\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod_{j\in J}\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{\text{\rm min}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[1]_{\Gamma,\overline{\Gamma}}^{\text{\rm min}}}
finite
\textstyle{{\mathrm{S}_{\overline{\Gamma}}^{\text{\rm min}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\prod_{j\in J}\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm min}},}}

together with canonical isomorphisms of line bundles

\begin{split}(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}})^{\otimes m_{0}}&\cong[1]_{(\Gamma,\Sigma),(\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Sigma})}^{{\text{\rm tor}},*}\bigl(\otimes_{j\in J}\bigl((\mathrm{S}_{\overline{\Gamma},\overline{\Sigma}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}},\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{*}(\underline{\det(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}})}^{\text{\rm can}})^{\otimes c_{j}}\bigr)\bigr)\\
&\cong\otimes_{j\in J}\bigl((\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}},\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{*}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}},\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{\log,d_{j}})^{\otimes c_{j}}\bigr)\\
&\cong\otimes_{j\in J}\bigl((\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}},\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{*}\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{*}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d_{j}})^{\otimes c_{j}}\bigr)\cong\textstyle\oint_{\Sigma}^{*}\,L,\end{split}

where
L:=(\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{\text{\rm min}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\overline{\Gamma}}^{\text{\rm min}})^{*}\bigl(\otimes_{j\in J}\bigl((\mathrm{S}_{\overline{\Gamma}}^{\text{\rm min}}\to\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm min}})^{*}(\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d_{j}})^{\otimes c_{j}}\bigr)\bigr)
is ample over
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma}^{\text{\rm min}}
because each of
\Omega_{\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}}^{\text{\rm min}}}^{d_{j}}
is ample over
\mathrm{S}_{\Gamma_{j}^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm min}}
, by Proposition
3.3.5
(
7
), as desired.
∎

When we apply Proposition
3.6.6
later in Section
5
, we will want the ratios
\frac{b_{j}}{a_{j}}
in Remark
3.6.5
to be as small as possible. Let us introduce the following:

Condition 3.6.7
.

\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},c}
is simply-connected.

Since the canonical homomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}\to\mathrm{H}^{{\text{\rm der}},c}
is a central isogeny, our assumption forces
\mathrm{H}^{\text{\rm der}}
to be also simply-connected. Then we have the following:

Proposition 3.6.8
.

Suppose Condition
3.6.7
holds. Then there exists an irreducible
W_{0}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
of positive parallel weight such that, for each
j\in J
, the pullback
W_{0,j}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}_{j})
of
W_{0}
satisfies
W_{0,j}^{\otimes{h}^{\vee}_{j}}\cong\det(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}})
, where
{h}^{\vee}_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
is the
dual Coxeter number
for the root system associated with any
\mathbb{C}
-simple factor of the complex semisimple Lie algebra
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{j,\mathbb{C}}

(
and any choice of Cartan subalgebra
)
.
(
Since
\mathrm{H}_{j}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-simple, this
{h}^{\vee}_{j}
is well defined, because the
\mathbb{C}
-simple factors of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{H}_{j,\mathbb{C}}
are all abstractly isomorphic to each other.
)
For each
j\in J
, this ratio
\frac{1}{{h}^{\vee}_{j}}
is the
smallest
among all
\frac{b_{j}}{a_{j}}
such that there exists some
W_{j}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}_{j})
such that
W_{j}^{\otimes a_{j}}\cong\det(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{j}^{\text{\rm std}})^{\otimes b_{j}}
for some
a_{j},b_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}

(
cf. Remark
3.6.5
)
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Lan16
, Thm. 3.8]
.
∎

4.
Geometric Sen theory for towers of Shimura varieties

In this section, we collect some known results about the
p
-adic geometry of towers of Shimura varieties, notably the differential equation satisfied by the locally analytic functions at the infinite level. This is a consequence of some calculation of the canonical Higgs field for towers of Shimura varieties, which was first discovered by the second author in the case of modular curves
[
Pan22
]
and in general done by Juan Esteban Rodríguez Camargo
[
RC22
]
.

4.1.
Shimura varieties and their compactifications

Let
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})
be a Shimura datum. In particular,
\mathrm{G}
is a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
, and
\mathsf{X}
is a conjugacy class of homomorphisms

(4.1.1)

h:\operatorname{Res}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{R}}

satisfying a list of axioms, which imply that the pair
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})
satisfies the assumptions in Section
3.1
. (See, e.g.,
[
Del79
, 2.1.1]
,
[
Mil05
, Def. 5.5 and Cor. 5.8]
, or
[
Lan
, Sec. 2.3]
. See also other parts of these references for many standard facts we review below.) In particular, we have the associated double coset spaces, their minimal compactifications, their toroidal compactifications, and the canonical algebraizations of all of these, as in Sections
3.2
and
3.3
, and they enjoy the properties we have shown in Sections
3.4
–
3.6
. We shall change the notation from
\mathrm{S}_{K}
etc to
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
etc in this subsection. By the theory of canonical models,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
admits a canonical model
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
over a number field
E\subset\mathbb{C}
, called the
reflex field
of the Shimura datum
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})
. We shall call
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
the canonical model of the Shimura variety associated with
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})
at level
K
. For simplicity, we shall also call various base changes of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
and their analytifications Shimura varieties. The precise meaning of our terminologies should be clear in the context.

By
[
Pin89
, 12.4]
, for a cofinal system of choices of
\Sigma
’s for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
, the corresponding toroidal compactifications
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\Sigma,\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
also admit canonical models
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
over
E
, and we shall call these the toroidal compactifications of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
. Without reviewing the conditions on
\Sigma
’s in
[
Pin89
, 12.4]
in detail, let us just note the following:

Remark 4.1.2
.

Given any projective
\Sigma
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
, its pullback
\Sigma^{\prime}
to any higher level
K^{\prime}\subset K
(which is, a priori, just for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime},\mathbb{C}}
) also qualifies as a projective cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}
, so that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is defined.

Each
h\in\mathsf{X}
as in (
4.1.1
) induces a homomorphism
h_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
, whose restriction to the first factor defines the so-called
Hodge cocharacter

(4.1.3)

\mu_{h}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}

(over
\mathbb{C}
). Essentially by the definition of a Shimura datum, the composition of (
4.1.3
) with the adjoint action of
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
on
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
induces the same decomposition as in (
3.1.1
), and the composition of (
4.1.3
) with the canonical homomorphism
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
recovers the
\mu^{\text{\rm ad}}
(defined by
y_{0}=h_{0}\in\mathsf{X}^{+}
) in (
3.1.2
). Hence, we can define
\mathrm{M}
,
\mathrm{N}
,
\mathrm{N}^{\text{\rm std}}
,
\mathrm{P}
, and
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}
as in (
3.1.3
) and the sentences preceding it; and define
\mathrm{Fl}:=\mathrm{P}\backslash\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}}:=\mathrm{H}_{\mathbb{C}}/\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}
as in (
3.1.5
).

Remark 4.1.4
.

The reflex field
E
is the field of definition of the conjugacy class of
\mu_{h}
, and both
\mathrm{Fl}
and
\mathrm{Fl}^{\text{\rm std}}
naturally have models over
E
because of this.

Remark 4.1.5
.

Given a representation
W
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P})
(resp.
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}})
), the action of
\operatorname{Lie}\mu_{h}
defines an increasing (resp. decreasing) filtration
\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}
on
W
by subrepresentations of
\mathrm{P}
(resp.
\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std}}
). However, the numbering of this filtration might differ from one defined by
\operatorname{Lie}\mu^{\text{\rm ad}}
in Remark
3.1.4
by a shifting, because the composition of
\operatorname{Lie}\mu^{\text{\rm ad}}
with
(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty}^{\text{\rm ad}})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset(\operatorname{Lie}K_{\infty})_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{M}
might differ from
\operatorname{Lie}\mu_{h}
by a nonzero homomorphism from
\mathbb{C}
to
\operatorname{Lie}Z(\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}})\cong\ker(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}\to\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}})
.

Remark 4.1.6
.

Everything in Sections
3.5
–
3.6
, including importantly Propositions
3.6.6
and
3.6.8
, specializes to the setting of Shimura varieties. We shall use the same notation
\underline{W}
,
\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}}
, etc, without further explanation.

In the remainder of this Section
4
, we fix a neat open compact subgroup
K=K^{p}K_{p}
of
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, with
K^{p}\subset\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}})
and
K_{p}\subset\mathrm{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Moreover, we fix a projective smooth cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
, and we shall write
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}=\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\Sigma}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, without emphasizing the choice of
\Sigma
. By Remark
4.1.2
, for each open subgroup
K^{\prime}\subset K
, the pullback
\Sigma^{\prime}
to level
K^{\prime}
is projective (but not necessarily smooth), and we shall similarly write
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}=\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, without emphasizing
\Sigma^{\prime}
. By Corollary
3.4.20
, the tower
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
indexed by open subgroups
K_{p}^{\prime}
of
K_{p}
, together with its canonical
K_{p}
-action, extends to a tower
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
; and the transition morphisms of this latter tower are finite Kummer étale surjective morphisms. Let
\widetilde{K_{p}}
be the quotient of
K_{p}
given by (
3.4.24
) (with
\mathrm{H}
there replaced with
\mathrm{G}
here). By Corollary
3.4.21
, the canonical actions of
K_{p}
on the towers
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
and
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
factor through faithful actions of
\widetilde{K_{p}}
. (In other words,
\widetilde{K_{p}}
is the image of
K_{p}
in the automorphism groups of these towers.)

4.2.
Hodge–Tate period morphisms

Now let us make the transition to
p
-adic geometry. Let
C
be the
p
-adic completion of an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. From now on, we fix an isomorphism
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
, whose restriction to
E\subset\mathbb{C}
induces an embedding
E\hookrightarrow C
and hence a
p
-adic place
v
of
E
. Let
E_{v}
denote the corresponding extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We shall denote by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,E_{v}}
etc (resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}
etc) the base changes of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
etc to
E_{v}
(resp.
C
). Note that these base changes (and therefore the constructions in the remainder of section) depend on
\iota
. We shall denote by
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K}
) the adic space associated with
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,E_{v}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}
) over
\operatorname{Spa}(E_{v},\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}})
(resp.
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
), and similarly denote other analytifications. Then we have compatible towers
\{\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
and
\{\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(E_{v},\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}})
, which base change to compatible towers
\{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
and
\{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
. Note that
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},C}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
satisfies the requirement as a
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-torsor over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}
in Construction
2.1.3
, and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}^{\text{\rm tor}}
satisfies the requirement for
j
in Construction
2.2.2
.

By
[
DLLZ23a
, Ex. 2.3.17]
, each
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
has a natural fs log structure defined by its boundary
\partial\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
divisor. By comparing étale local descriptions of log smooth and log étale morphisms in the algebraic setup in
[
Kat89
]
with those in the
p
-adic analytic setup in
[
DLLZ23a
]
, we see that the transition morphisms of the tower
\{\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}}
are finite Kummer étale surjective morphisms. Hence,
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is a
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-torsor which defines a pro-Kummer étale cover of
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
in
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. By base change to
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
, we obtain a
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-torsor
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, which defines a pro-Kummer étale cover of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
.

This last
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-torsor over
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
has an underlying topological space

\mathcal{S}_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}|\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}|

equipped with the inverse limit topology, where
|\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}|
denotes the underlying topological space of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. Concretely, the topology of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
has a basis consisting of
\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}\subset K_{p}^{\prime}}|(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})|
, with
K_{p}^{\prime}
an open subgroup of
K_{p}
and
\mathcal{U}
an affinoid open subspace of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, where
\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to|\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}|
denotes the natural projection morphism. There is a natural continuous action of
K_{p}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
which factors through the quotient
\widetilde{K_{p}}
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

We define the sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{+}:=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{+}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
to be the restriction of the integral completed structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{+}
to the underlying topological space of
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, and define
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{+}[\frac{1}{p}]
. Explicitly,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{+}
sends any
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}|\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})|
as above to
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}^{+}(\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}))
, where
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})
is viewed as an object in the pro-Kummer étale site
\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. There is a similar description of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
in terms of the completed structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}
. Both sheaves are
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-equivariant, and there is a natural morphism of ringed sites
\pi_{K_{p}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})\to(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}})
.

Remark 4.2.2
.

Intuitively, we think of the ringed site
(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})
as a “
Shimura variety of infinite level at
p
”. One caveat is that, while this ad hoc definition will be enough for our purpose, the definition of the structural sheaf here might not be the most correct one in general. From the point of view of
perfectization
in
[
Bha
, Sec. 4.1]
, it is unclear whether there should be some derived structure on the sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, unless we know that
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is perfectoid.

It was first observed by Scholze in
[
Sch15
]
that any Hodge-type Shimura variety of finite level at
p
has a Hodge–Tate period morphism towards the associated flag variety, which played a fundamental role in his study of the
p
-adic geometry of Shimura varieties. (See
[
PS16
,
Lan22
]
for the analogous statement for toroidal compactifications.) Using Diao–Lan–Liu–Zhu’s work on (
p
-adic) logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert correspondence and its comparison with Deligne’s (complex) Riemann–Hilbert correspondence on all Shimura varieties (see
[
DLLZ23b
, Thm. 1.5]
), one can define Hodge–Tate period morphism for general Shimura varieties (cf.
[
RC22
, Thm. 4.2.1]
). To state the result, we introduce some notation.

Let
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
denote the adic space associated with the partial flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}_{C}=\mathrm{P}_{C}\backslash\mathrm{G}_{C}
(cf. (
3.1.5
)). From this quotient expression, with any representation
W
of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
, increasingly filtered as in Remark
4.1.5
, we can canonically associate an increasingly filtered
\mathrm{G}_{C}
-equivariant vector bundle
(\underline{W}_{\mathrm{Fl}_{C}},\text{\rm Fil}_{\bullet})
on
\mathrm{Fl}_{C}
. By (
p
-adic) analytification, we obtain the associated (increasingly) filtered
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant vector bundle
(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell},\text{\rm Fil}_{\bullet})
on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. The assignment
W\mapsto(W_{\mathcal{F}\ell},\text{\rm Fil}_{\bullet})
defines a tensor functor from
\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{P}_{C})
to the category of increasingly filtered vector bundles on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. Such a construction extends naturally to representations of quotient (algebraic) groups of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
, including, in particular,
\mathrm{P}_{C}^{c}
,
\mathrm{M}_{C}
, and
\mathrm{M}_{C}^{c}
.

As in Remark
4.1.6
, with each
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{P}^{\text{\rm std},c})
, we also have an associated (decreasingly) filtered vector bundle
(\underline{W},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
, together with its
canonical extension

(\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. By using the chosen isomorphism
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
, and by
p
-adic analytification, we obtain a filtered vector bundle
(\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
on
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. The assignment
W\mapsto(\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}},\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
defines a tensor functor from
\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{P}_{C}^{\text{\rm std},c})
to the category of decreasingly filtered vector bundles on
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
.

Theorem 4.2.3
.

There is a
K_{p}
-equivariant morphism of ringed sites

\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}),

called the
Hodge–Tate period morphism
, such that, for each
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
, there is a
K_{p}
-equivariant isomorphism of locally free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
-modules

\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{{\text{\rm tor}},*}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\pi_{K_{p}}^{*}(\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}}).

Moreover,
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is deduced from a
K_{p}
-equivariant morphism
(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{+})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{+})
by inverting
p
.

Remark 4.2.4
.

To make the isomorphism also equivariant with respect to the action of local Galois group of
E_{v}
, one needs to put appropriate Tate twists by the Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{h}
in the isomorphism (cf.
[
RC22
, Thm. 4.2.1]
and also Remark
4.1.5
).

Proof of Theorem
4.2.3
.

This is essentially
[
RC22
, Thm. 4.2.1]
. See also
[
BP21
, Thm. 4.4.40]
. Let us still give a sketch here. As explained in
[
DLLZ23b
, Sec. 5.2]
, any finite-dimensional
V\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{G}^{c})
, viewed as a representation of
\widetilde{K_{p}}
via
\widetilde{K_{p}}\to K_{p}^{c}\subset\mathrm{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, defines an (automorphic)
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-étale local system
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
, which has
unipotent geometric monodromy
along the boundary (in any toroidal compactification) and is
de Rham
by
[
LZ17
, Thm. 1.2]
. Therefore, by extending
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
to a Kummer étale
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local system
{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
over
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, by applying the algebraized
p
-adic logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert functor
D_{\text{\rm dR},\log}^{\text{\rm alg}}
as in
[
DLLZ23b
, Sec. 4.1]
, and by base change from
E_{v}
to
C
