, we obtain a filtered log connection over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. By
[
DLLZ23b
, Thm. 5.3.1]
, this last filtered log connection is canonically isomorphic to
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V_{\mathbb{C}}}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{C},\iota}C
, where
V_{\mathbb{C}}:=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p},\iota^{-1}}\mathbb{C}
and
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V_{\mathbb{C}}}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
is as in the last paragraph of Section
3.5
. Moreover, such canonical isomorphisms are compatible with tensor products and duals. In the following, we shall denote by
(\mathcal{V}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
the (
p
-adic) analytification of
({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V_{\mathbb{C}}}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{C},\iota}C
over
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. Let
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
denote the pullback of
{}_{\text{\rm k\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
to
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. (Its further pullback to
\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is the
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}
constructed in Construction
2.2.2
.) Since
{}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
is de Rham and has unipotent geometric monodromy along the boundary,
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
on
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\text{\rm can}},\nabla,\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet})
are associated in the sense that there is a natural isomorphism

{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR},\log}\cong\mathcal{V}^{\text{\rm can}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR},\log}

over
\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, which is compatible with filtrations and log connections on both sides (see the paragraph preceding
[
DLLZ23b
, Sec. 3.5]
and the notation for period sheaves there). For simplicity, let us write
X=\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. Similar to
[
Sch13a
, Prop. 7.9]
, one defines an increasing
Hodge–Tate
filtration on
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
using the relative position of the
\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR}}^{+}
-lattices
\mathbb{M}={}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR}}^{+}
and
\mathbb{M}_{0}=({}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR},\log}^{+})^{\nabla=0}
in
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR}}
; i.e.,

\text{\rm Fil}_{-j}({}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}):=(\mathbb{M}\cap\text{\rm Fil}^{j}\mathbb{M}_{0})/(\text{\rm Fil}^{1}\mathbb{M}\cap\text{\rm Fil}^{j}\mathbb{M}_{0}),

where both
\mathbb{M}
and
\mathbb{M}_{0}
are equipped with the
\ker(\theta)
-adic topology, and where
\theta:\mathbb{B}_{\text{\rm dR}}^{+}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
denotes the usual
\theta
map. Moreover, we have

\operatorname{gr}_{j}({}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=(\operatorname{gr}^{j}{}_{\text{\rm dR}}\underline{V})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}\textit{h}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-j).

Since the Hodge filtration
\text{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{V}^{\text{\rm can}}
is induced (via
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
) by the decreasing filtration on
V_{\mathbb{C}}|_{\mathrm{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm std},c}}
, which is defined up to conjugation by
\mathrm{G}^{c}
by the action of
\operatorname{Lie}\mu_{h}
, or rather just
\mu_{h}
, as in Remark
4.1.5
, it follows from the above that the Hodge–Tate filtration on
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is associated with the opposite parabolic subgroup
\mathrm{P}_{C}^{c}
(up to conjugation). Moreover, the formation of such filtrations are compatible with the formation of tensor products and duals.

Consequently, the Tannakian
\mathrm{G}_{C}^{c}
-torsor
\mathcal{G}^{c}
on
(X,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
given by the tensor functor
\bigl(V\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{G}^{c})\bigr)\mapsto{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
admits a
\mathrm{P}_{C}^{c}
-reduction, by
[
Mil90
, Prop. 1.7]
(based on
[
SR72
, Ch. IV, especially IV.2.2.5]
). (Here the torsors are only defined by the Tannakian formalism, which makes sense over any ringed site.) Since
{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{V}
is canonically trivialized on the pro-Kummer étale cover
Y:=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, so is the restriction of
\mathcal{G}^{c}
to
Y
. By the usual universal property of the algebraic partial flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}_{C}
, the
\mathrm{P}_{C}^{c,\text{\rm an}}
-reduction induces a morphism of ringed sites

(Y,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{Y})\to(\mathrm{Fl}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Fl}_{C}}).

Since
{\mathcal{F}\ell}\cong\mathrm{Fl}_{C}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(C)}\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
(as in
[
Hub94
, Prop. 3.8 and Rem. 4.6]
), this factors through a morphism of ringed sites

(Y,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{Y})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}),

whose restriction to the underlying topological space
|Y|=\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
gives the desired
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, which is deduced from a morphism
(Y,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{+}|_{Y})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{+})
by inverting
p
. All the claimed properties then follow from the construction.
∎

Remark 4.2.5
.

In the construction of
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}
, one can use the associated adjoint Shimura datum
(\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm ad}},\mathsf{X}^{\text{\rm ad}})
instead of
\mathrm{G}^{c}
. Let
K^{\text{\rm ad}}
denote the image of
K
in
\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm ad}}({\mathbb{A}^{\infty}})
, which is neat as
K
is. Up to refinement of cone decompositions for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
, as in Proposition
3.4.14
, we can choose smooth projective toroidal compactifications
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\text{\rm ad}}}
with normal crossing boundary divisors as before such that the finite map
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\text{\rm ad}}}
extends to a proper morphism between toroidal compactifications
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. (We shall adopt a similar notation system for various objects defined by the similarly defined tower over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
.) Then we obtain a natural morphism of ringed sites

\phi:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})\to(\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\text{\rm ad}},p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\text{\rm ad}}}),

where
(\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\text{\rm ad}},p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\text{\rm ad}}})
is the analogue of
(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\text{\rm ad}}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. The Hodge–Tate period morphisms for both the source and target of
\phi
have the same target. We claim that the Hodge–Tate period morphism for
(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})
factors through
\phi
. This follows from the construction and from the compatibility of the logarithmic
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert functor for local systems with unipotent geometric monodromy under pull-back, by
[
DLLZ23b
, Thm. 3.2.3(4)]
.

We will need a “
locally analytic
” version of the Hodge–Tate period morphism.

Definition 4.2.6
.

We define the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
as the subsheaf of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
of
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-locally analytic sections. Concretely, for any open subgroup
K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}
and any affinoid
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, we know that
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}\bigl(\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})\bigr)=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{+}(\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}))[\frac{1}{p}]
is a
p
-adic Banach space representation of
K_{p}^{\prime}
, and we define
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}\bigl(\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})\bigr)
to be the subspace of
K_{p}^{\prime}
-locally analytic vectors of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}))
. This defines a
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-equivariant subsheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. (See also
[
RC22
, Def. 6.2.1]
.)

Note that
\pi_{K_{p}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})\to(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}})
induces a morphism

\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})\to(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}),

because all the sections of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}
are
K_{p}
-invariant, and hence
K_{p}
-locally analytic.

Theorem 4.2.7
.

The Hodge–Tate period morphism
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
in Theorem
4.2.3
induces a
K_{p}
-equivariant morphism of ringed sites

\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})

such that, for
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
, there is a
K_{p}
-equivariant isomorphism of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
-modules

\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})\cong\pi^{\text{\rm la},*}_{K_{p}}(\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}}).

Proof.

The morphism
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})
gives a morphism of sheaves of rings
(\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{\#}:(\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. We claim that this morphism factors through
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
and therefore defines a morphism
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la}}:(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})\to({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})
. This is a purely local question. Suppose that
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}=\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})
is an open subset of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
with
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
affinoid and
K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}
an open subgroup, and that
\mathcal{V}\subset{\mathcal{F}\ell}
is an affinoid containing
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}})
. Note that such
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}
’s form a basis of the topology of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. Since
\mathcal{V}
is quasi-compact, it is stable under the action of an open subgroup of
K_{p}
, which we may assume to be
K_{p}^{\prime}
up to shrinking it. Then
(\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{\#}
gives a continuous map of
p
-adic Banach spaces

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}(\mathcal{V})\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}),

which is
K_{p}^{\prime}
-equivariant. Since
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
is of finite type over
C
, the action of
K_{p}^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{+}(\mathcal{V})/p
factors through a finite quotient. Hence, the action of
K_{p}^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}(\mathcal{V})
is locally analytic, by
[
Pan25b
, Prop. 2.7]
.

Now let
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
. By Theorem
4.2.3
, there is a
K_{p}
-equivariant isomorphism

(\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})\otimes_{(\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm tor}})^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cong\pi_{K_{p}}^{-1}(\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}})\otimes_{\pi_{K_{p}}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}})}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}.

We need to show this remains an isomorphism if we replace
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
with
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
. Again, this is a local question, and we shall use the same notation as above. Up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
if necessary, we may assume that
\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}}|_{\mathcal{U}}
is a trivial vector bundle, and that
\underline{W}_{\mathrm{Fl}_{C}}
is trivial on a Zariski open subset
\tilde{V}
of
\mathrm{Fl}_{C}
whose associated adic space contains
\mathcal{V}
. Choose a basis of
\mathcal{W}^{\text{\rm can}}(\mathcal{U})
and a basis of
\underline{W}_{\mathrm{Fl}_{C}}|_{\tilde{V}}
, viewed as a basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}(\mathcal{V})
. Then the above isomorphism corresponds to a matrix
M\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}))
, where
n=\dim_{C}(W)
, and it suffices to show that
M\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}))
. Note that the actions of
K_{p}^{\prime}
on the two bases we choose are locally algebraic. Therefore,
M
and
M^{-1}
have entries in the subspace of
K_{p}^{\prime}
-locally algebraic vectors of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}})
, and hence in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}})
.
∎

Remark 4.2.8
.

Similar to Remark
4.2.5
,
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la}}
factors through its analogue for the adjoint Shimura datum as well.

Remark 4.2.9
.

The ringed site
(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})
should be thought of as a “
toroidal compactification of the locally analytic Shimura variety of infinite level at
p
”. Even though this ad hoc definition looks very similar to the one in Definition
4.2.1
, we will see in Theorem
4.3.2
below that the issue mentioned in Remark
4.2.2
will disappear when we replace
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}}
with
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
.

4.3.
Geometric Sen theory

The goal of this subsection is to describe the differential equation satisfied by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
in terms of the geometry of
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
and relate
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
to the construction in the previous section.

Consider the sequence of inclusions of Lie algebras

\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{C}\subset\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}\subset\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C},

where
\mathrm{N}_{C}
is the unipotent radical of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
, as in Sections
3.1
and
4.1
. The group
\mathrm{P}_{C}
acts on each term by the adjoint action. Hence, this sequence of Lie of algebras defines a sequence of
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant vector bundles

\mathfrak{n}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{C}\mathfrak{g}

on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. Here
\mathfrak{g}:=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}
, and the last equality follows as the
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-action on
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}
can be extended to the adjoint action of
\mathrm{G}_{C}
. Concretely, for any
C
-point
x
of
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
defining a parabolic subgroup
\mathrm{P}_{x}
of
\mathrm{G}_{C}
conjugate to
\mathrm{P}_{C}
, with unipotent radical
\mathrm{N}_{x}
, the subspaces
\mathfrak{n}_{x}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{x}^{0}=\mathfrak{g}
are given by
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}_{x}\subset\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{x}\subset\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}=\mathfrak{g}
. By taking the derivation of the
G
-action on the flag variety, we see that
\mathfrak{g}
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{g}^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{C}\mathfrak{g}
has a nature structure of a Lie algebroid on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, with Lie bracket

[f_{1}\otimes l_{1},f_{2}\otimes l_{2}]=f_{1}l_{1}(f_{2})\otimes l_{2}-f_{2}l_{2}(f_{1})\otimes l_{1}+f_{1}f_{2}\otimes[l_{1},l_{2}],

for any
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
and any
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathfrak{g}
. The kernel of the natural anchor map from
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
to the tangent bundle of
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
is exactly
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
.

Since the action of
K_{p}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
is locally analytic, it induces a
C
-linear action of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
. By extending this action
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
-linearly, we obtain an action of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{C}\mathfrak{g}=\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathfrak{g}^{0})
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
. We will focus on restrictions of this action to
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathfrak{n}^{0})
and
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathfrak{p}^{0})
. The following result was first established in the case of modular curves by the second-named author
[
Pan22
]
and in general by Juan Esteban Rodríguez Camargo
[
RC22
]
.

Theorem 4.3.1
.

The action of
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathfrak{n}^{0})
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
is zero.

Proof.

This is
[
RC22
, Cor. 6.2.13]
. The basic idea is that, by the general result in geometric Sen theory, the locally analytic vectors
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
are annihilated by the geometric Sen operators of the tower
\{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}
of Shimura varieties, which can be computed via the
p
-adic variations of Hodge structures associated with automorphic local systems. The main results of
[
DLLZ23b
]
identify such
p
-adic variations of Hodge structures with the tautological complex variations of Hodge structures over Shimura varieties, and from this identification, it follows that the geometric Sen operators are essentially given by the actions of
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathfrak{n}^{0})
.
∎

Next, we relate
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}}
to the constructions in Section
4.2
. Recall that the tower
\{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}\}_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}
defines a
\widetilde{K_{p}}
-torsor in
\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm pro\'{e}t}}
. Let
\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the LB space of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-valued locally analytic functions on the
p
-adic Lie group
\widetilde{K_{p}}
. It is a representation of
\widetilde{K_{p}}
by the left translation action, and can be written as an inductive limit of unitary
p
-adic Banach space representations
\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\varinjlim_{n}\mathscr{C}_{n}
. This can be seen by taking a normal uniform pro-
p
subgroup
K_{0}
of finite index in
\widetilde{K_{p}}
, which exists by
[
DdSMS99
, Cor. 8.34]
; and by considering
\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}})
as the direct limit of
K_{0}^{p^{n}}
-analytic functions on
\widetilde{K_{p}}
. By applying Construction
2.2.2
to each
\mathscr{C}_{n}
, and by taking direct limit, we obtain a pro-Kummer étale local system

{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}=\varinjlim_{n}{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}_{n}}

on
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, which is independent of the choice of
\mathscr{C}_{n}
. Accordingly, consider

{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}:=\varinjlim_{n}\bigl({}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}_{n}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}\bigr),

where the completed tensor product means
p
-adically completed tensor product. Denote by
\eta_{K_{p}}:\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
the natural projection morphism of sites.

Theorem 4.3.2
.

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism

R\eta_{K_{p},*}\bigl({}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}}\bigr)\cong\pi_{K_{p},*}^{\text{\rm la}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})

of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}
-modules on
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
.

Proof.

This is
[
RC22
, (6.23)]
. Essentially, one needs to show that the left-hand side is concentrated in degree zero. The key point is that
H^{*}
of the left-hand side can be calculated via an explicit log Higgs complex involving geometric Sen operators.
∎

Corollary 4.3.3
.

Let
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
be one-dimensional and of positive parallel weight, as in Definition
3.6.4
and Remark
3.6.5
. Consider the associated
\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}^{\text{\rm tor}}
, as in Remark
4.1.6
, with analytification
\mathcal{L}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
. Then we have

R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})\bigr)\in D^{\geq d}(C),

where
d=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathsf{X})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{S}_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}}=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}|\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}^{\text{\rm tor}}|
, we can apply
[
Sta
,
Tag 0EXJ
]
and write
\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})
as the colimit of the pullback sheaves of
\pi_{K^{\prime}_{p},*}^{\text{\rm la}}\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})
, for
K^{\prime}_{p}\subset K_{p}
open. Hence,
R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})\bigr)
is the colimit of
R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime},*}^{\text{\rm la}}\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})\bigr)
, by
[
Sta
,
Tag 09YP
]
; and it suffices to show that, for any
K^{\prime}_{p}
,

R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{K_{p}^{\prime},*}^{\text{\rm la}}\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})\bigr)\in D^{\geq d}(C).

In fact, we may assume that
K^{\prime}_{p}=K_{p}
, because
\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})
is independent of the level
K_{p}
, by Remark
3.5.1
. By Theorem
4.3.2
and by applying the projection formulas twice, we obtain

\begin{split}&R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{K_{p},*}^{\text{\rm la}}\pi_{K_{p}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}^{-1})\bigr)\cong R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{K_{p},*}^{\text{\rm la}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\bigr)\\
&\cong R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}},R\eta_{K_{p},*}({}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\bigr)\\
&\cong R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K,\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}^{\text{\rm tor}},{}_{\text{\rm prok\'{e}t}}\underline{\mathscr{C}^{\text{\rm la}}(\widetilde{K_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\eta_{K_{p}}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\bigr).\end{split}

Thus, this corollary follows, by applying Corollary
2.2.5
with
X_{C}=\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}^{\text{\rm tor}}
,
L_{C}=\underline{W}^{\text{\rm can}}
,
U=\mathrm{Sh}_{K,C}
, and
V=\mathscr{C}_{n}
, where the condition (
2.1.9
) in Theorem
2.1.8
and Corollary
2.2.5
are satisfied because of Proposition
3.6.6
; and by taking direct limit over
n
, which is applicable because
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\text{\rm tor}}
is proper over
C
.
∎

The following summarizes what we have learned so far from studying both the complex and
p
-adic geometry of Shimura varieties:

Theorem 4.3.4
.

Let
\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la}}
be as in Theorem
4.2.7
, and let

(4.3.5)

\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}:=R\pi_{\text{\rm HT},*}^{\text{\rm la}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{\rm la}})

in
D^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})
, the bounded derived category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
-modules on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
acts naturally on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
when the latter is viewed as an object in the bounded derived category of sheaves of abelian groups on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, which is compatible with the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
-module structure; i.e., we have
l\cdot(fs)=l(f)s+f(l\cdot s)
, for all
l\in\mathfrak{g}
,
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, and
s\in\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
. This action extends
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
-linearly to an action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
. Moreover, we have the following:

(1)

The action of
\mathfrak{n}^{0}
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}

(
defined by restricting that of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
)
is zero.

(2)

Let
W\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
be one-dimensional and of
positive parallel weight
, as in Definition
3.6.4
and Remark
3.6.5
, with associated
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}:=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
over
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. Then we have

H^{<d}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1})=0.

From now on, we will drop the superscript “
\mathbb{L}
” in the notation of derived tensor product of
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
and vector bundles on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
.

Proof.

Part (
1
) follows from (
4.3.5
) and Theorem
4.3.1
. Also, by (
4.3.5
) and the projection formula, we have
R\Gamma({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1})\cong R\Gamma\bigl(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}^{\text{\rm tor}},\pi_{\text{\rm HT}}^{\text{\rm la},*}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1})\bigr)
. Based on this, Part (
2
) follows from Theorem
4.2.7
and Corollary
4.3.3
.
∎

4.4.
Horizontal action

Finally, we discuss the induced action of the Levi quotient
\mathfrak{m}^{0}\cong\mathfrak{p}^{0}/\mathfrak{n}^{0}
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
. Note that
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
annihilates
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, because it is the kernel of the anchor map of the Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
. Hence, the restriction of the Lie bracket of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
to
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
-bilinear. Equivalently,
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
is the
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant bundle associated with the
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-representation
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}
, and this Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{p}^{0}
agrees with the one coming from the Lie bracket on
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}
(viewed as a map
\wedge^{2}\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}\to\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}
). Let
U(\mathfrak{m}^{0})
be the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{m}^{0}
over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. Equivalently,
U(\mathfrak{m}^{0})
is the
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant bundle associated with the universal enveloping algebra
U(\mathfrak{m})\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{M}_{C})
, where
\mathfrak{m}:=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{M}_{C}
. The action of
\mathfrak{m}^{0}
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
extends naturally to
U(\mathfrak{m}^{0})
. In particular, we get an action on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
of the center

Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\cong U(\mathfrak{m})^{\mathrm{M}_{C}}\subset H^{0}({\mathcal{F}\ell},U(\mathfrak{m}^{0})).

of
U(\mathfrak{m})
. On the other hand, since the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
acts on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
, the center of the universal enveloping algebra
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
also acts on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
. To describe the relation between these two actions, we recall the Harish-Chandra homomorphism. (We also take this opportunity to explain our choices of conventions.) Fix a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}
inside of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}
, and let
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
denote the Weyl group of
\mathfrak{g}
with respect to
\mathfrak{h}
. We choose compatible positive roots of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{m}
, and denote by
\rho
and
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
the usual half-sums of positive roots of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{m}
, respectively. There is an isomorphism of
C
-algebras

(4.4.1)

\gamma:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

called the
Harish-Chandra homomorphism
, where the action of
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on
U(\mathfrak{h})
is centered at
0
. It has the property that:

(4.4.2)

for any irreducible representation
V\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{G}_{C})
of highest weight
\lambda
, the center
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
acts on
V
via
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\xrightarrow{\gamma}U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\to U(\mathfrak{h})\xrightarrow{\lambda+\rho}C
.

Similarly, there is the Harish-Chandra isomorphism of
C
-algebras

\gamma_{\mathfrak{m}}:Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}},

where
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
denotes the Weyl group of
\mathfrak{m}
with respect to
\mathfrak{h}
, viewed as a subgroup of
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
. Consider the representation
\det\mathfrak{n}
of
\mathrm{M}_{C}
, whose weight we shall denote by
\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}
. Since
\det\mathfrak{n}
is one-dimensional, by the theory of highest weights, all rational multiples of
\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}
, including
\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}
, are
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-invariant. Let
T_{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}}:U(\mathfrak{h})\to U(\mathfrak{h})
denotes the translation by
-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}
; i.e., the induced map
T_{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}}^{*}:\operatorname{Spec}U(\mathfrak{h})\to\operatorname{Spec}U(\mathfrak{h})
is the translation by
-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}
when restricted to points defined by
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. Then there is an injection
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
which makes the following diagram commute:

(4.4.3)

\textstyle{{Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{{Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\mathfrak{m}}}
\textstyle{{U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}}}
\textstyle{{U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}}}}

Theorem 4.4.4
.

The action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
factors through
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
via
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

This result was previously obtained by Juan Esteban Rodríguez Camargo, and probably well known to experts. We may choose the positive roots such that all the roots in
\mathfrak{n}
are positive. In this case, we have
\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}=\rho-\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

The first step is to produce a map
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
compatible with both actions on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
. Consider the quotient of
U(\mathfrak{g})
by its right ideal
\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})
. Let
\mathfrak{p}:=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}
. By the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt theorem, the natural homomorphism of algebras
U(\mathfrak{m})\cong U(\mathfrak{p})/\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{p})\to U(\mathfrak{g})/\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})
is injective. We shall view
U(\mathfrak{m})
as a subalgebra of
U(\mathfrak{g})/\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})
via this injection.

Lemma 4.4.5
.

The image of the composition of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-equivariant maps

Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to U(\mathfrak{g})\to U(\mathfrak{g})/\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})

lies in
U(\mathfrak{m})
. The induced map
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to U(\mathfrak{m})^{\mathrm{M}_{C}}=Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
agrees with
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.4.5
.

For the first part, it suffices to show that

Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\subset U(\mathfrak{m})+\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g}).

Consider the action of the Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{h}
as in (
4.1.3
) which gives (via
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
) a weight decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{n}^{\text{\rm std}}\oplus\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{n}
(compatible with (
3.1.1
), as explained in Section
4.1
), with
\mathfrak{m}
being the subspace of weight
0
. Using this decomposition, we can see that the weight-
0
part of
U(\mathfrak{g})
is contained in
U(\mathfrak{m})+\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})
. The desired claim then follows as
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
is fixed by
\mu_{h}
.

For the second part, we need to recall the construction of the Harish-Chandra isomorphism
\gamma
. Our choice of the positive roots defines a Borel subalgebra
\mathfrak{b}
of
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
\mathfrak{u}:=[\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{b}]
be its unipotent radical. Then Harish-Chandra showed that

Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\subset U(\mathfrak{h})+\mathfrak{u}U(\mathfrak{g}).

Moreover, the right-hand side is a direct sum and induces a map
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to U(\mathfrak{h})
which gives
T_{-\rho}\circ\gamma
. Similarly, the choice of positive roots defines a Borel subalgebra
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathfrak{m}
with unipotent radical
\mathfrak{u_{\mathfrak{m}}}:=[\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}}]=\mathfrak{u}\cap\mathfrak{m}
. We have

Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\subset U(\mathfrak{h})+\mathfrak{u_{\mathfrak{m}}}U(\mathfrak{m}),

which induces
T_{-\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}\circ\gamma_{\mathfrak{m}}
in a similar way. By putting these together, we obtain

Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))+\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})\subset U(\mathfrak{h})+\mathfrak{u_{\mathfrak{m}}}U(\mathfrak{m})+\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})\subset U(\mathfrak{h})+\mathfrak{u}U(\mathfrak{g}),

which induces
\gamma^{\prime}:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
in the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{T_{-\rho}\circ\gamma}
\textstyle{{Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{-\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}\circ\gamma_{\mathfrak{m}}}
\textstyle{{U(\mathfrak{h})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{U(\mathfrak{h})}}

Since
\rho=\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}+\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
, this induced map
\gamma^{\prime}
indeed agrees with
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
, as desired.
∎

Back to the proof of Theorem
4.4.4
. Let
U(\mathfrak{g}^{0}):=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{C}U(\mathfrak{g})
. We can define a natural
C
-algebra structure on it, which extends the algebra structures of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
and
U(\mathfrak{g})
, and satisfies the usual relation

lf=fl+l(f),

for
l\in\mathfrak{g}
and
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
. Since
\mathfrak{n}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{0}
is a Lie ideal and annihilates
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, we see that

U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})\mathfrak{n}^{0}U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})=\mathfrak{n}^{0}U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})=\mathfrak{n}^{0}U(\mathfrak{g})

is an ideal of
U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})
. It is
\mathrm{G}_{C}
-equivariant, and under the dictionary between
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant vector bundles over
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
and representations of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
, the inclusion
\mathfrak{n}^{0}U(\mathfrak{g})\subset U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})
corresponds to
\mathfrak{n}U(\mathfrak{g})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
.

From the universal property of universal enveloping algebras, we obtain a natural morphism
U(\mathfrak{p}^{0})\to U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})
, which is nothing but the morphism of
\mathrm{G}(C)
-equivariant bundles associated with the natural map
U(\mathfrak{p})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-representations. By Lemma
4.4.5
, the image of

Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{C}Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{C}U(\mathfrak{g})\to U(\mathfrak{g^{0}})/\mathfrak{n^{0}}U(\mathfrak{g})

is contained in the image of the injective map

Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\to U(\mathfrak{m^{0}})\cong U(\mathfrak{p^{0}})/\mathfrak{n^{0}}U(\mathfrak{p^{0}})\subset U(\mathfrak{g^{0}})/\mathfrak{n^{0}}U(\mathfrak{g}),

and the induced map
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
agrees with
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
. This proves the theorem because, by Theorem
4.3.4
(
1
), the natural action of
U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
(extending that of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
) factors through the quotient
U(\mathfrak{g}^{0})/\mathfrak{n}^{0}U(\mathfrak{g})
.
∎

5.
Translation functors

In this section, we explain how to deduce a vanishing result from Theorem
4.3.4
, by using translation functors to “untwist
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
”. The argument is pretty standard and goes back to the first paper of Beilinson–Bernstein on localizations
[
BB81
]
.

We shall proceed with the same setting as in Section
4
. In particular, we shall continue to identity algebraic objects over
\mathbb{C}
with algebraic objects over
C
via the chosen isomorphism
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
.

5.1.
Sufficiently regular weights

Recall that we have fixed a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
of
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}
inside of
\mathfrak{p}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{P}_{C}
, and denoted by
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
the Weyl groups of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{m}
, respectively, with respect to
\mathfrak{h}
. Moreover, we have the Harish-Chandra isomorphism
\gamma:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
with respect to the natural action of
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
(centered at
0\in\mathfrak{h}
), as in (
4.4.1
). Based on
\gamma
, a character
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
(i.e., a
C
-homomorphism) will be identified with a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit
[\lambda]
in the space
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
of weights
\lambda:\mathfrak{h}\to C
. Similarly, based on the Harish-Chandra isomorphism
\gamma_{\mathfrak{m}}:Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
, a character
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\to C
will be identified with a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-orbit
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
in
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

For simplicity, let us introduce the following:

Definition 5.1.1
.

We say a root of
\mathfrak{g}
is a
shorter root
if it is shorter than some other root in the same
C
-simple factor of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Remark 5.1.2
.

Shorter roots only exist on
C
-simple factors of
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B
or
C
.

Definition 5.1.3
.

We say that a
\mathbb{C}
-simple factor of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
, or rather of its (semisimple) derived subalgebra
[\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}},\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}]\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm sc}}\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm der}}\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
(see Lemma
3.4.2
and the paragraph preceding it), is
compact
(resp.
noncompact
) if the pullback of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{N}
to it is zero (resp. nonzero). Accordingly, we say that a
\mathbb{C}
-simple factor of
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm sc}}
or
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is
compact
(resp.
noncompact
) if its Lie algebra is a compact (resp. noncompact) factor of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
. With any choice of
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
, we will say that the corresponding
C
-simple factor of
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}
, its derived subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}=[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}^{\text{\rm sc}}\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{C}^{\text{\rm ad}}
,
\mathrm{G}_{C}^{\text{\rm sc}}
, or
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is
\iota
-compact
(resp.
\iota
-noncompact
).

Remark 5.1.4
.

By the definition of
\mathrm{N}
in Section
3.1
, a
\mathbb{C}
-simple factor of
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
,
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm sc}}\cong\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
,
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm sc}}
, or
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\text{\rm ad}}
is compact if and only if it acts trivially on
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbb{C}}
. However, since
\mathrm{Fl}
has a natural model over
E\subset\mathbb{C}
(see Remark
4.1.4
), the notions of
\iota
-compact and
\iota
-noncompact factors of
\mathfrak{g}
depend on the embedding
E\hookrightarrow C
induced by
\iota
in general. We will take advantage of this flexibility in the following.

Definition 5.1.5
.

Let
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
be any isomorphism.

(1)

We say a character
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\to C
or its kernel is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular
if, for every weight
\lambda^{\prime}
in the corresponding
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-orbit
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
and every root
\alpha
in
\mathfrak{n}
, with its coroot denoted by
{\alpha}^{\vee}
as usual, we have

(\lambda^{\prime}-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}},{\alpha}^{\vee})\not\in\begin{cases}\{-1,-2\},&\text{if $\alpha$ is a shorter root, as in Definition \ref{def-shorter-rt}};\\
\{-1\},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(2)

Similarly, we say a character
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
or its kernel is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular
if, for every weight
\lambda^{\prime}
in the corresponding
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit
[\lambda]
and every root
\alpha
in
\mathfrak{n}
(or, equivalently, in all
\iota
-noncompact
C
-simple factors of
\mathfrak{g}
—see Remark
5.1.6
below), we have

(\lambda^{\prime},{\alpha}^{\vee})\not\in\begin{cases}\{-1,-2\},&\text{if $\alpha$ is a shorter root, as in Definition \ref{def-shorter-rt}};\\
\{-1\},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(3)

We say a character
Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))\to C
or its kernel is
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular
if its
C
-linear extension
Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C\cong Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota^{\prime}}
-sufficiently regular for some isomorphism
\iota^{\prime}:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
.

Remark 5.1.6
.

It will follow from our explicit descriptions in Sections
5.3
and
5.4
that every root
\alpha
in an
\iota
-noncompact
C
-simple factor of
\mathfrak{g}
(see Definition
5.1.3
) is in the
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit of some root of some noncompact factor of
\mathfrak{n}
, because each nonzero factor of
\mathfrak{n}
contains both the longest and shortest roots. Note that, once we consider such
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbits, the signs in Definition
5.1.5
are no longer important, and this is why we did not use negative signs in the condition (
1.1.1
) in the introduction.

Example 5.1.7
.

Definition
5.1.5

(
1
)
and
(
3
)
in some cases of type
\mathrm{A}_{1}
:

(1)

(
Modular curves.
)

(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})=(\mathrm{GL}_{2},\mathcal{H}^{\pm})
, where
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}=\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
is the union of the upper and lower Poincaré half-planes. In this case,
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is trivial, and a character
\lambda^{\prime}
of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))=U(\mathfrak{m})
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular if and only if
\lambda^{\prime}|_{\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)}\neq 0
, where
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)=[\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C),\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)]
is the derived subalgebra. A character
[\lambda]
of
Z(U(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})))
is
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular if and only if
[\lambda]|_{Z(U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})))}
is not the character
\chi_{0}
of the trivial representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
.

(2)

(
Shimura curves.
)
Let
F
be a finite totally real field extension of
\mathbb{Q}
, and
D/F
a quaternion algebra split at exactly one place
v_{0}|\infty
. Fix any isomorphism
D\otimes_{F}F_{v_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and consider the Shimura datum
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})=(\operatorname{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}D^{\times},\mathcal{H}^{\pm})
, where
(\operatorname{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}D^{\times})(\mathbb{R})\cong\prod_{v|\infty}(D\otimes_{F}F_{v})^{\times}
acts on
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
via the isomorphism
(D\otimes_{F}F_{v_{0}})^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
induced by the above fixed one. In this case,
\mathfrak{g}\cong\oplus_{\tau:F\to C}(D\otimes_{F,\tau}C)\cong\oplus_{\tau:F\to C}\,\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}
, where each factor
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}
is a copy of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)
indexed by
\tau
, containing its derived subalgebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}
. Via any
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
, the embeddings
F\hookrightarrow F_{v_{0}}\cong\mathbb{R}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
induce an embedding
\tau_{0}:F\to C

(
depending on
\iota
)
; and a character
\lambda^{\prime}
of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular if and only if
\lambda^{\prime}|_{\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)^{\tau_{0}}}\neq 0
. A character
[\lambda]
of
Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))
is
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular if and only if its
C
-linear extension
[\lambda]\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C:Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C\cong\otimes_{\tau:F\to C}\,Z(U(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}))\to C
is not
\chi_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C
when restricted to
Z(U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}))
in
at least one
factor
Z(U(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}))
.
(
This last notion makes no use of any isomorphism
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
, and hence there is no
\tau_{0}
associated with the distinguished place
v_{0}|\infty
of
F
.
)

(3)

(
Hilbert modular varieties.
)
Let
F
be a finite totally real field extension of
\mathbb{Q}
. Consider the Shimura datum
(\mathrm{G},\mathsf{X})=(\operatorname{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{GL}_{2,F},\prod_{\tau:F\to\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}^{\pm})
. As in
(
2
)
,
\mathfrak{g}\cong\oplus_{\tau:F\to C}\,\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}
; and we have
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}\subset\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}
, for each
\tau
. A character
\lambda^{\prime}
of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular if and only if
\lambda^{\prime}|_{\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}}\neq 0
for all
\tau
, and hence this notion is independent of the choice of
\iota
. A character
[\lambda]
of
Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))
is
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular if and only if
[\lambda]\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C:Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C\cong\otimes_{\tau:F\to C}\,Z(U(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}))\to C
is not
\chi_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C
when restricted to
Z(U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}))
in
every
factor
Z(U(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(C)^{\tau}))
.

Definition 5.1.8
.

The
non-
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular

locus
of
\operatorname{Spec}Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
(resp.
\operatorname{Spec}Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
) is the (necessarily reduced) Zariski closure of the closed points corresponding to non-
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular kernels of characters as in Definition
5.1.5
. We denote by
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota}\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
(resp.
\mathcal{I}^{\iota}\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
) the (necessarily radical) ideal defining this locus. Equivalently, it is the intersection of all the non-
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular kernels of characters
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\to C
(resp.
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
). Similarly, we define the ideal
\mathcal{I}
of
Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))
as the intersection of the kernel of all the non-
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular characters.

Remark 5.1.9
.

Let us choose positive roots of
\mathfrak{g}
, with usual half-sum of positive roots
\rho
. Then
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
acts on any irreducible
V_{\lambda}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{G}^{c})
of highest weight
\lambda
via the infinitesimal character
[\lambda+\rho]
. Since
(\rho,{\alpha}^{\vee})=1
for all positive roots
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{g}
, it follows that, if
\lambda
is sufficiently regular (via
\iota^{-1}:C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbb{C}
) in the sense of
[
Lan16
, Thm. 4.10]
, then
[\lambda+\rho]
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficient regular in the sense of Definition
5.1.5
(
2
). The converse is true in most cases, except for the following two issues:

(1)

On
\iota
-noncompact factors of types
\mathrm{B}
and
\mathrm{C}
, the condition in
[
Lan16
, Thm. 4.10]
does not distinguish between shorter and other (longer) roots, while the condition in Definition
5.1.5
(
2
) does. Nevertheless, the statement and proof in
[
Lan16
, Thm. 4.10]
can be improved to take care of this difference.

(2)

We have the flexibility in choosing
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
and hence permuting the
C
-simple factors of
\mathfrak{g}
, which provides additional flexibility when
\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
admits nontrivial compact factors (see Definition
5.1.3
and Remark
5.1.4
).

Proposition 5.1.10
.

Consider the finite homomorphism
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
in
(
4.4.3
)
.

(1)

\mathcal{I}^{\iota}=(\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}})^{-1}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota})
. Equivalently,
f:Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular if and only if every extension
f^{\prime}:Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\to C
of
f
via
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
is
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular.

(2)

Every homomorphism
Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to C
can be extended to a
\mu_{h}^{\iota}
-sufficiently regular character
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\to C
via
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
.

(3)

\mathcal{I}
is the radical ideal of
Z(U(\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}))\cap\sum_{\iota^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}^{\iota^{\prime}}
, where
\iota^{\prime}
runs through all isomorphisms
\iota^{\prime}:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
and the sum is a finite sum.

Proof.

As for (
1
), just note that the shift by
-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}
in Definition
5.1.5
cancels the corresponding shift in the construction of
\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}
. We omit the details here. As for (
2
), it suffices to show that, given a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit
[\lambda]
in
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, we can find a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-orbit
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}\subset[\lambda]
such that, for every
\lambda^{\prime}\in[\lambda]
and every root
\alpha
in
\mathfrak{n}
, we have

(\lambda^{\prime},{\alpha}^{\vee})\not\in\begin{cases}\{-1,-2\},&\text{if $\alpha$ is a shorter root};\\
\{-1\},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We may assume that
(\lambda^{\prime},{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\mathbb{Q}
, for all roots
\alpha
in
\mathfrak{g}
and
\lambda^{\prime}\in[\lambda]
, by choosing a
\mathbb{Q}
-basis
e_{1}=1,e_{2},\cdots,e_{r}
of the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space spanned by all
(\lambda^{\prime},{\alpha}^{\vee})
in
C
, and by considering the projection of
[\lambda]
onto its
e_{1}
-component. Let us choose the positive roots of
\mathfrak{g}
such that the roots in
\mathfrak{n}
are positive, and choose
\lambda^{\prime}\in[\lambda]
in the dominant Weyl chamber. Hence,
(\lambda^{\prime},{\alpha}^{\vee})\geq 0
, for every positive root
\alpha
. Since roots in
\mathfrak{n}
are
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-invariant,
(w(\lambda^{\prime}),{\alpha}^{\vee})\geq 0
, for all
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and roots
\alpha
in
\mathfrak{n}
. Thus, we can take
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda^{\prime})
. Finally, for the last part, let
L\subset\mathbb{C}
be a sufficiently large finite extension of
E
over which all
\mathbb{C}
-simple factors are defined. Then it follows that
\mathcal{I}^{\iota}
only depends on
\iota|_{L}:L\to C
. Then our claim follows from Definition
5.1.5
.
∎

We now explain where the sufficient regularity condition comes from and how we are going to use it. For technical reasons, let us assume that the following holds:

Condition 5.1.11
.

\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm der}}
is simply-connected, and
\mathrm{G}=\mathrm{G}^{c}
.

In this case,
\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm sc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{G}^{\text{\rm der}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{G}^{{\text{\rm der}},c}
, and so Condition
3.6.7
holds, with
\mathrm{H}
there replaced with
\mathrm{G}
here. Let
W_{0}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{M}^{c})
be any one-dimensional representation as in Proposition
3.6.8
, and let
\lambda_{0}
denote the weight of
W_{0}
. Concretely, in the notation of
[
Lan16
, Sec. 3.3]
(via
\iota:\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C
), we have the following pullbacks of
\lambda_{0}
on individual
\iota
-noncompact

C
-simple factors of
\mathrm{G}_{C}^{\text{\rm sc}}
(see Definition
5.1.3
):

•

Type
\mathrm{A}
:
(1,\ldots,1;0,\ldots,0)\pmod{(1,\ldots,1;1,\ldots,1)}
.

•

Types
\mathrm{B}
and
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{R}}
:
(1;0,\ldots,0)
.

•

Type
\mathrm{C}
:
(1,1,\ldots,1)
.

•

Type
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{H}}
:
(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\ldots,\frac{1}{2})
.

•

Types
\mathrm{E}_{6}
and
\mathrm{E}_{7}
:
(0,\ldots,0,\frac{2\sqrt{3}}{3})
and
(1,0,\ldots,0,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2})
.

In
[
Lan16
, Sec. 3.3]
, it was also shown (by working case-by-case on all possible
C
-simple factors of
\mathrm{G}_{C}^{\text{\rm sc}}
) that
(\lambda_{0},{\alpha}^{\vee})=-1
for every root
\alpha
in
\mathfrak{n}
unless
\alpha
is a shorter root as in Definition
5.1.1
, in which case
(\lambda_{0},{\alpha}^{\vee})=-2
. This explains the appearance of
-2
in the definition of
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular weights.

Still assuming Condition
5.1.11
, consider the finite-dimensional irreducible representation
V_{0}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{G}_{C})
of extremal weight
\lambda_{0}
; i.e.,
\lambda_{0}
is in the
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit of the highest weight of
V_{0}
. We shall also denote by
V_{0}
the induced
\mathfrak{g}
-representation.

Proposition 5.1.12
.

Let
\lambda_{0}
and
V_{0}
be as above. Then a character of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
is
\mu_{h}
-
sufficiently regular
as in Definition
5.1.5
if and only if the corresponding
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-orbit
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
satisfies that, for all weights
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
of
V_{0}
other than
\lambda_{0}
, we have

(5.1.13)

([\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}-\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{0}^{\prime})\cap\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}([\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}})=\emptyset.

Equivalently,
(\operatorname{Id}-w)(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}})\neq\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
for all
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
, all
\lambda\in[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
, and all weights
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\neq\lambda_{0}
of
V_{0}
.

The proof of Proposition
5.1.12
will be given in Sections
5.3
and
5.4
.

5.2.
Vanishing results

Recall that, in Theorem
4.3.4
, we defined
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\in D^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell})
, with natural actions of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
and
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
d=\dim_{C}({\mathcal{F}\ell})=\dim(\mathrm{Sh}_{K})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathsf{X})
.

Theorem 5.2.1
.

Assume that Condition
5.1.11
and Proposition
5.1.12
hold. Then
H^{<d}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}})
is annihilated by
(\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota})^{n}
, for some
n>0
.

Corollary 5.2.2
.

Under the same assumptions,
H^{<d}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}})
is annihilated by
(\mathcal{I}^{\iota})^{n}
, for some
n>0
.

Proof.

Combine Theorem
5.2.1
with Proposition
5.1.10
and Theorem
4.4.4
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
5.2.1
uses an action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes_{C}Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
. For simplicity, let us drop the subscript
C
in the remainder of this subsection. In Section
4.4
, we constructed an action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
, which is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
-linear and commutes with the
U(\mathfrak{g})
-action. Let
W
be a finite-dimensional representation of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
, with associated
\mathfrak{g}
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
over
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, as in Section
4.2
. We define an action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
on
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
with:

•

Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
acting only on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
; and

•

Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
acting diagonally on the tensor product.

The upshot is that, for any map
W\to W^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-representations, the induced map
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\to\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
is equivariant with respect to this action.

If
W
is a representation of
\mathrm{M}_{C}
, then
\mathfrak{n}^{0}
annihilates
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
, and we obtain a diagonal action of
\mathfrak{m}^{0}\cong\mathfrak{p}^{0}/\mathfrak{n}^{0}
on
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
, and this gives an action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\subset H^{0}({\mathcal{F}\ell},U(\mathfrak{m}^{0}))
, as explained in Section
4.4
. Accordingly, we define an action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
on
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
, where the first (resp. second)
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
acts only on
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
(resp. diagonally). It follows from the proof of Theorem
4.4.4
that the previous
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
-action factors through this via

\operatorname{Id}\otimes\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}}:Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{m})).

When
W=C
is the trivial representation, the action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
factors through the diagonal quotient
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
. For general
W
, by
[
BG80
, Thm. 2.5]
, which generalizes an earlier work of Kostant’s, the action of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
on
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}
is zero when restricted to the ideal
\mathcal{J}_{W}^{\Delta}\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
defined as follows. Under the Harish-Chandra isomorphism
\gamma_{\mathfrak{m}}:Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U(\mathfrak{h})^{\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
, elements in
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
can be regarded as
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}\times\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-invariant polynomial functions on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. Then
\mathcal{J}_{W}^{\Delta}
consists of functions
Q
such that:

•

Q(\chi+\mu,\chi)=0
for any weight
\mu
of
W
and
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

Denote by
\mathcal{J}_{W}\subset Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
the ideal
(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\gamma^{\mathfrak{m}})^{-1}(\mathcal{J}_{W}^{\Delta})
. Recall that we introduced the representation
V_{0}\in\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{G}_{C})
of extremal weight
\lambda_{0}
in the paragraph preceding Proposition
5.1.12
. Let
W_{0}
be the one-dimensional representation of
\mathrm{M}_{C}
of (highest) weight
\lambda_{0}
, as before.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

Suppose
W
is any nontrivial representation of
\mathrm{M}_{C}
appearing as a subquotient of
V_{0}\otimes_{C}{W}^{\vee}_{0}

(
viewed as a representation of
\mathrm{P}_{C}
)
. Then the composition of
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Id}\otimes 1}Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\to\bigl(Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\bigr)/(\mathcal{J}_{W}+\mathcal{J}_{C})
factors through
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))/(\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota})^{n}
, for some
n>0
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota}
was introduced in Definition
5.1.8
as the ideal defining the non-
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular locus of
\operatorname{Spec}Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
. By Hilbert’s Nullstellensatz, it suffices to prove the assertion at the level of maximal ideals. Via the Harish-Chandra isomorphism, a closed point of
\operatorname{Spec}\bigl(\bigl(Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))\bigr)/(\mathcal{J}_{W}+\mathcal{J}_{C})\bigr)
corresponds to a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-orbit
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
and a
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-orbit
[\chi]
in
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
satisfying:

•

\lambda^{\prime}+\mu-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}\in[\chi]
, for some
\lambda^{\prime}\in[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
and weight
\mu
of
W
; and

•

\lambda^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}\in[\chi]
, for some
\lambda^{\prime}\in[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Hence,

([\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}}+\mu)\cap\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}([\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{\det\mathfrak{n}})\neq\emptyset,

for some weight
\mu
of
W
. By our assumption,
\mu=-\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
, for some weight
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
of
V_{0}
. Thus, by Proposition
5.1.12
,
[\lambda]_{\mathfrak{m}}
is not
\mu_{h}
-sufficiently regular.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.2.1
.

Consider the
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-equivariant surjection

V_{0}\twoheadrightarrow W_{0},

with kernel
W^{\prime}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}
(resp.
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime}
) be the line bundle (resp. vector bundle) on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
associated with
W_{0}
(resp.
W^{\prime}
) in
\operatorname{Rep}_{C}(\mathrm{P}_{C})
, as in Section
4.2
. Then we have a
\mathfrak{g}
-equivariant exact sequence of the corresponding vector bundles on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}

0\to\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\otimes_{C}V_{0}\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}\to 0.

By taking the tensor product with
\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}
, we obtain an exact triangle

\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}\otimes_{C}V_{0}\to\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\xrightarrow{+1}

equivariant with respect to the
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
-action introduced before. By Proposition
3.6.8
, Remark
4.1.6
, and Theorem
4.3.4
, we have

H^{<d}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}\otimes_{C}V_{0})\cong H^{<d}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1})\otimes_{C}V_{0}=0.

Hence, for
i<d
, the cohomology of the above exact triangle gives a short exact sequence

0\to H^{i}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}})\to H^{i+1}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime}).

Note that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime}
is filtered by equivariant vector bundles on
{\mathcal{F}\ell}
associated with nontrivial irreducible
\mathrm{P}_{C}
-subquotients
W
of
V_{0}\otimes{W}^{\vee}_{0}
. Therefore, its cohomology
H^{i+1}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{-1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}\ell}^{\prime})
is filtered by
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
-modules annihilated by
\mathcal{J}_{W}
, for some such
W
. Since
\mathcal{J}_{C}
annihilates
H^{i}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}})
, the above short exact sequence implies that
H^{i}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}})
is filtered by
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))\otimes Z(U(\mathfrak{g}))
-modules annihilated by
\mathcal{J}_{W}+\mathcal{J}_{C}
. Thus, by Lemma
5.2.3
, the
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))
-action on
H^{i}({\mathcal{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}^{\text{\rm la}})
factors through
Z(U(\mathfrak{m}))/(\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota})^{n}
, for some
n>0
, as desired.
∎

Remark 5.2.4
.

The proof shows that, to make the exponent of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota}
in Theorem
5.2.1
explicit, it suffices to understand the exponent of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\iota}
in Lemma
5.2.3
.

5.3.
Justification of the key observation

In order to prove Proposition
5.1.12
, we need to show that a weight
\lambda:\mathfrak{h}\to C
satisfies
\lambda-w(\lambda)=\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
, for some
1\neq w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}
and weight
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
of
V_{0}
other than
\lambda_{0}
, if and only if there exists some root
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{n}
such that
(\lambda,{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\{-1,-2\}
or
\{-1\}
depending on whether
\alpha
is a shorter root as in Definition
5.1.1
or not. Since the roots in
\mathfrak{n}
are chosen to be negative in
[
Lan16
]
, to reduce the number of negative signs, we shall switch from
\mathfrak{n}
to the opposite algebra
\mathfrak{n}^{\text{\rm std}}
, and consider the equivalent condition that there exists some root
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{n}^{\text{\rm std}}
such that
(\lambda,{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\{1,2\}
or
\{1\}
depending on whether
\alpha
is a shorter root as in Definition
5.1.1
or not. Since
\mathrm{G}
is reductive and
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathrm{G}(C)
, we have
\mathfrak{g}\cong\mathfrak{z}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}
, where
\mathfrak{z}=Z(\mathfrak{g})
is the center of
\mathfrak{g}
, and
\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}=[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]\cong\oplus_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\,\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}
, where the
\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}
’s are its
C
-simple factors (cf. Definition
5.1.3
). Accordingly, we have decompositions
\mathfrak{m}\cong\mathfrak{z}\oplus(\oplus_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\,\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon})
,
\mathfrak{h}\cong\mathfrak{z}\oplus(\oplus_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\,\mathfrak{h}_{\upsilon})
,
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\prod_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
,
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\prod_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon}}
, etc. (We shall use similar subscripts “
\upsilon
” for other similar decompositions, without explicitly introducing them.) In particular, we have
\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}\cong\oplus_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\,\mathfrak{h}_{\upsilon}
and
\mathfrak{h}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}\cong\oplus_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\,\mathfrak{h}_{\upsilon}
. Based on these decompositions, let us write
\lambda|_{\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}}=(\lambda_{\upsilon})_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}
,
\lambda_{0}|_{\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}}=(\lambda_{0,\upsilon})_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}
, and
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}|_{\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}}=(\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime})_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}
. Since
V_{0}
is irreducible,
\lambda_{0}\neq\lambda_{0}^{\prime}
(as weights of
\mathfrak{m}\cong\mathfrak{z}\oplus(\oplus_{\upsilon\in\Upsilon}\,\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon})
) if and only if
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}\neq\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
(as weights of
\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon}
) for some
\upsilon\in\Upsilon
. So it suffices to prove the following:

Proposition 5.3.1
.

In the above, for each
\upsilon\in\Upsilon
, a weight
\lambda_{\upsilon}:\mathfrak{h}_{\upsilon}\to C
satisfies
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
, for some
1\neq w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
and weight
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
of
V_{0}|_{\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon}}
other than
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}
, if and only if there exists some root
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
such that
(\lambda,{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\{1,2\}
or
\{1\}
depending on whether
\alpha
is a shorter root as in Definition
5.1.1
or not.

On each
C
-simple factor
\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon}
of
\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\text{\rm der}}
as above, the weights
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
of
V_{0,\upsilon}:=V_{0}|_{\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon}}
are integral in the sense that it paired integrally with the coroots of
\mathfrak{m}_{\upsilon}
. Moreover, when we view them as subsets of the corresponding real vector spaces spanned by all integral weights, they lie in the convex hull of the
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
-orbit of
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}
. Given the explicit description of
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}
, there is a very short list of all weights
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
’s of
V_{0,\upsilon}
.

On classical factors, given our explicit knowledge of
\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
as combinations of sign changes and permutations, we can easily verify by case-by-case arguments, which we shall explain based on the types of
\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}
in the following Sections
5.3.1
–
5.3.5
. As for the exceptional factors, since there are only two possible cases, we shall resort to brute force computation, and we shall explain our methods in Section
5.4
.

5.3.1.
Type
\mathrm{A}_{n}

In this case, by using the same notation system as in
[
Lan16
, Sec. 3.3.1]
, we have the following:

•

\lambda_{0,\upsilon}=\varpi_{r_{\upsilon}}\equiv(1,\ldots,1;0,\ldots,0)\pmod{(1,\ldots,1;1,\ldots,1)}
, where the semicolon “
;
” occurs between the
r_{\upsilon}
-th and
(r_{\upsilon}+1)
-th entries;

•

\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}\cong S_{n+1}
(the permutation group on
n+1
elements); and

•

\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
can be any vector with entries in
\{0,\pm 1\}
, with
+1
’s only occurring before “
;
” and the
-1
’s only occurring after “
;
”, and with the number of
+1
’s and
-1
’s being nonzero and equal to each other. (See, e.g., the explicit descriptions on representations of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1}(\mathbb{C})
in
[
FH91
, §§15.2–15.3]
.)

Hence,
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
, where
\lambda_{\upsilon}=(\lambda_{\upsilon,1},\ldots,\lambda_{\upsilon,n+1})
, exactly when all the following hold:

•

(\lambda_{\upsilon},e_{i}-e_{w(i)})=\lambda_{\upsilon,i}-\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i)}\in\{0,1\}
, for
1\leq i\leq r_{\upsilon}
;

•

(\lambda_{\upsilon},e_{i}-e_{w(i)})=\lambda_{\upsilon,i}-\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i)}\in\{0,-1\}
, for
r_{\upsilon}<i\leq n+1
; and

•

the numbers of
+1
’s and
-1
’s in the above are nonzero and equal to each other.

Assuming that all of these hold, we would like to show that
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=1
for some root
\alpha
of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
. Note that the roots of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
are
e_{i_{1}}-e_{i_{2}}
, for all
1\leq i_{1}\leq r_{\upsilon}<i_{2}\leq n+1
, and there are no shorter roots. Hence, it suffices to verify the claim that there exist some
1\leq i_{1}\leq r_{\upsilon}<i_{2}\leq n+1
such that
\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{1}}-\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{2}}=(\lambda_{\upsilon},e_{i_{1}}-e_{i_{2}})=1
.

Let
i_{0}
be any integer such that
1\leq i_{0}\leq r_{\upsilon}
and
\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{0}}-\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i_{0})}=1
, which exists by assumption. Consider its orbit under the action of powers of
w
(as elements of
S_{n}
). Let
N_{0}
denote the cardinality of this orbit. For any integer
N
such that
0\leq N<N_{0}
, we define the
integer-valued
function

F(N):=\sum_{j=0}^{N}(\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{j}(i_{0})}-\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{j+1}(i_{0})})=\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{0}}-\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{N+1}(i_{0})}.

By the choice of
i_{0}
, we have
F(0)=1
. Since
w^{N_{0}}(i_{0})=i_{0}
, we have
F(N_{0}-1)=0
. Note that
F(N)-F(N-1)\in\{0,1\}
when
1\leq w^{N}(i_{0})\leq r_{\upsilon}
, and
F(N)-F(N-1)\in\{0,-1\}
when
r_{\upsilon}<w^{N}(i_{0})\leq n+1
. In particular, when the variable
N
increases by
1
from
N-1
, the value of
F
can either increase by
1
, stay the same, or decrease by
1
. The value can increase only when
1\leq w^{N}(i_{0})\leq r_{\upsilon}
, and can decrease only when
r_{\upsilon}<w^{N}(i_{0})\leq n+1
. Hence, we may assume that there exists some
N_{1}
such that
1\leq w^{N_{1}}(i_{0})\leq r_{\upsilon}
and
F(N_{1})\geq F(N)
, for all
0\leq N<N_{0}
. In particular,
F(N_{1})\geq F(0)=1
. Since
F(N_{0}-1)=0<F(N_{1})
, the value of
F
has to decrease by
1
at some point; i.e., there exists some
N_{2}>N_{1}
such that
r_{\upsilon}<w^{N_{2}}(i_{0})\leq n+1
,
\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{N_{2}}(i_{0})}-\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{N_{2}+1}(i_{0})}=-1
, and
F(N_{2})=F(N_{1})-1
. If
1\leq w^{N_{2}+1}(i_{0})\leq r_{\upsilon}
, we set
i_{1}:=w^{N_{2}+1}(i_{0})
and
i_{2}:=w^{N_{2}}(i_{0})
. Otherwise, and we set
i_{1}:=w^{N_{1}}(i_{0})
and
i_{2}:=w^{N_{2}+1}(i_{0})
. In both cases, we have
1\leq i_{1}\leq r_{\upsilon}<i_{2}\leq n+1
and
\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{1}}-\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{2}}=(\lambda_{\upsilon},e_{i_{1}}-e_{i_{2}})=1
, justifying the above claim.

Conversely, suppose
\alpha=e_{i}-e_{j}
, for some
1\leq i\leq r_{\upsilon}<j\leq n+1
, is a root of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
, with coroot
{\alpha}^{\vee}=e_{i}-e_{j}
; and suppose
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=\lambda_{\upsilon,i}-\lambda_{\upsilon,j}=1
. Let
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
be the simple reflection with respect to
\alpha
. Then
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})\alpha=\alpha=e_{i}-e_{j}=\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
, if we take
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}=w(\lambda_{0,\upsilon})
in this case.

We shall write
\lambda_{\upsilon}=(\lambda_{\upsilon,1},\ldots,\lambda_{\upsilon,n})
in the remaining classical cases.

5.3.2.
Type
\mathrm{B}_{n}

In this case, by using the same notation system as in
[
Lan16
, Sec. 3.3.2]
, we have the following:

•

\lambda_{0,\upsilon}=\varpi_{1}=(1;0,\ldots,0)
;

•

\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}\cong S_{n}\ltimes\{\pm 1\}^{n}
, and we shall denote by
\operatorname{sgn}(w,i)
the sign of
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
at the
i
-th factor of
\{\pm 1\}^{n}
; and

•

\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
is either
(2;0,\ldots,0)
or
(1;0,\ldots,0)
or
(1;0,\ldots,0,\pm 1,0,\ldots,0)
. (See, e.g., the explicit description on the so-called
standard representation
of
\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}(\mathbb{C})
in
[
FH91
, §18.1 and §19.4]
.)

Hence,
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
exactly when one of the following three cases holds:

(1)

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(1)}=2
; and

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,i}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,i)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i)}=0
, for all
1<i\leq n
.

(2)

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(1)}=1
; and

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,i}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,i)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i)}=0
, for all
1<i\leq n
.

(3)

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(1)}=1
;

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,i_{0}}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,i_{0})\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i_{0})}=\pm 1
, for exactly one
1<i_{0}\leq n
; and

•

\lambda_{\upsilon,i}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,i)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(i)}=0
, for all
i\neq 1,i_{0}
.

Assuming that one of the above cases holds, we would like to show that
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\{1,2\}
or
\{1\}
depending on whether
\alpha
is a shorter root or not. Note that the roots of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
are
e_{1}
and
e_{1}\pm e_{i}
, for all
1<i\leq n
, and
e_{1}
is the only shorter root.

In case (
1
), consider the orbit of
1
under the action of powers of
w
(as elements of
S_{n}
). Let
N_{1}
denote the cardinality of this orbit. For all
1\leq j<N_{1}
, since
w^{j}(1)\neq 1
, we have
\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{j}(1)}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,w^{j}(1))\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{j+1}(1)}=0
, or rather
\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{j}(1)}=\operatorname{sgn}(w,w^{j}(1))\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w^{j+1}(1)}
. Since we cannot have
\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\lambda_{\upsilon,1}=2
, we must have
\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(1)}=\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\prod_{0\leq j<N_{1}}\operatorname{sgn}(w,w^{j}(1))\,\lambda_{\upsilon,1}=\lambda_{\upsilon,1}+\lambda_{\upsilon,1}=2
, in which case
\operatorname{sgn}(w,w^{j}(1))=-1
for an odd number of exponents
0\leq j<N_{1}
. If we consider the root
\alpha=e_{1}
of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
, with coroot
{\alpha}^{\vee}=2e_{1}
, then
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=2\lambda_{\upsilon,1}=2
.

In cases (
2
) and (
3
), if
w(1)=1
, in which case
\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)=-1
and
2\lambda_{\upsilon,1}=1
, and if we consider the root
\alpha=e_{1}
of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
, with coroot
{\alpha}^{\vee}=2e_{1}
, then
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=2\lambda_{\upsilon,1}=1
. Otherwise, if
w(1)\neq 1
, and if we consider the root
\alpha=e_{1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,e_{w(1)}
of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
, with coroot
{\alpha}^{\vee}=e_{1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,e_{w(1)}
, then
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=\lambda_{\upsilon,1}-\operatorname{sgn}(w,1)\,\lambda_{\upsilon,w(1)}=1
.

In all three cases, we have
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\{1,2\}
or
\{1\}
depending on whether
\alpha
is a shorter root or not.

Conversely, suppose
\alpha
is a root of
\mathfrak{n}_{\upsilon}^{\text{\rm std}}
.

•

Suppose
\alpha=e_{1}
, with
{\alpha}^{\vee}=2e_{1}
. Let
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
denote the simple reflection with respect to
\alpha
. Then
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})\alpha
. If
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=(\lambda_{\upsilon},2e_{1})=2
, then
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=(2;0,\ldots,0)
, and we are in case (
1
) above. If
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=(\lambda_{\upsilon},2e_{1})=1
, then
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=(1;0,\ldots,0)
, and we are in case (
2
) above.

•

Suppose
\alpha=e_{1}\pm e_{i}
for some
1<i\leq n
and some sign
\pm
, with
{\alpha}^{\vee}=e_{1}\pm e_{i}
. Suppose
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})=1
. Let
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
denote the simple reflection with respect to
\alpha
. Then
\lambda_{\upsilon}-w(\lambda_{\upsilon})=(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})\alpha=\alpha=e_{1}\pm e_{i}=\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
, if we take
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}=w(\lambda_{0,\upsilon})
in this case, and we are in case (
3
) above.

These show that, if
(\lambda_{\upsilon},{\alpha}^{\vee})\in\{1,2\}
or
\{1\}
depending on whether
\alpha
is a shorter root or not, then we are in one of the three cases (
1
), (
2
), and (
3
) above, for some
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
and weight
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
of
V_{0,\upsilon}
other than
\lambda_{0,\upsilon}
.

5.3.3.
Type
\mathrm{C}_{n}

In this case, by using the same notation system as in
[
Lan16
, Sec. 3.3.3]
, we have the following:

•

\lambda_{0,\upsilon}=\varpi_{n}=(1,1,\ldots,1)
;

•

\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}\cong S_{n}\ltimes\{\pm 1\}^{n}
, and we shall denote by
\operatorname{sgn}(w,i)
the sign of
w\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\upsilon}}
at the
i
-th factor of
\{\pm 1\}^{n}
; and

•

\lambda_{0,\upsilon}-\lambda_{0,\upsilon}^{\prime}
